No criterion ticked: no scheduler runs these functions, no control commit or `expired`, `reminder_due` event is raised (FRW-030, FRW-036), and the store of the raised count and the server clock are the service's. The anchor of `firstAfterDays` is a parameter, not a default (Open questions). Decisions in `docs/adr/0249-lane-FRW-027-039.md` (decision 17, proposed).

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18):** All six criteria are ticked. Decisions, none the owner's, in `docs/adr/0389-w4-FRW-034-expire-and-remind.md` (proposed): the reminders count from the participant's activation; both timers are needed; a backlog of due reminders is one event; the count of raised reminders is kept apart from the resource and moved before the event, so a crash between the two loses a reminder and never repeats one; an `expired` workflow is `locked` with no fill (`lockFor('expired')`, which FRW-029 had left open); a `correcting` workflow does not expire; the sweep runs every minute. Outside the lane: `packages/authority/src/index.ts` exports the schedule functions and `lockFor` now answers for `expired`; the operation `remindWorkflow` (`packages/protocol/src/definition/frw.ts`, generated `spec/dep`, `spec/permissions`, the matrix, `packages/tck/test/pending.ts`, `tests/security/test/sec-045-route-sweep.test.ts`, `packages/protocol/test/definition.test.ts`); `docs/spec/12` §6.2 and §10; migration 0076 with the purge list (`document-purge-pg.ts`) and `schema.pg.test.ts`. Here the sweep is called directly; the scheduler's own slot job is tested under BE-052.

### FRW-035 Push workflow state to connected clients

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 12 §10 (real-time plane); 05 §5.2, §5.5; 07 §4 · **Requirements:** R13, R3 · **Depends on:** FRW-025, PRO-032

As a maintainer, I want the workflow state and the participant's status pushed to connected clients, so that the editor changes what a person may do without a reload.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a channel is opened on a document with a workflow, then `ch.opened` carries the `workflow` object with its state and the participant's status.
- [x] Given the workflow state or the participant's status changes, then `wf.changed` (server to client) carries the new state and the participant's status, sent in the same way as `perm.changed` (a complete new object).
- [x] Given a transition that changes what a connection may do (activation, pause, completion, lock), then `access` is recomputed, its `rev` increases and `perm.changed` is sent as well; the server enforces the new permissions from the moment it computes them.
- [x] Given a client that was disconnected during a transition, when it reconnects, then `ch.opened` brings it to the current state.
- [x] Given a message to a participant, then it carries that participant's own status and not other participants' personal details.
- [x] Given the editor receives `wf.changed`, then the guided bar and lock indicator update without a reload.

**Evidence:** `packages/authority/test/frw-035.workflow-push.test.ts` (vitest, Node.js 22.18, 11 tests; lane w4, 2026-11-17): the real `Room` with a workflow record built by `sendWorkflow`, `openWorkflow` and `completeParticipant`. Criterion 1: `ch.opened.workflow` is the state and the connection's own participant status (`active`, `waiting`, `copy`; the state alone for a connection that is no participant; no member without a workflow). Criteria 2 and 3: `Room.workflowChanged(record, perms)` sends `perm.changed` (revision 2, `access.fill.active` recomputed from the record, not from the stale token claim) and then `wf.changed` with the complete new object to each connection whose own object changed, none to a connection whose object did not; the revision is written as a control commit; the order 2 participant's fill is refused before and accepted after the transition. Criterion 4: a channel opened after the transition, on the same room or on a room started with the record, gets the current object and access in `ch.opened`. Criterion 5: no other participant's id, `sub` or roles appears in any message. `packages/protocol/src/bridge/frw-035.workflow-wire.test.ts` (3 tests): `RoomBridge.opened` puts `workflow` into the DEP `ch.opened`; `wf.changed` is translated and sent after the held `perm.changed` of the same transition (both after its control commit), or at once when no access is held; the payloads pass the generated `ChannelOpened` and `WorkflowStatus` schemas. Criterion 6 (lane w4, second pass, 2026-11-18): `packages/backend-dep/src/frw-035.workflow-port.universal.test.ts` (5 tests, Node.js 22.18; the workflow port of `backend-dep`, present while `workflow` is negotiated, reports the object of `ch.opened` and each `wf.changed` in order, tells a later listener the latest status once, keeps a status that arrived before anyone listened, stops after unsubscribe, and reads and acts through the workflow routes with the bearer token); `packages/element/test/frw-035-wf-changed.test.ts` (3 tests: the lock indicator's state moves at once on each push, though the participant's read of the resource is refused, and the guided bar's summary says `waiting` or `paused` from the participant status and shows no reason once active); `packages/ui/e2e/frw-035-wf-changed.spec.ts` (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 3 passed, run on this machine on 2026-11-18: the shell's `FillBar` changes its text from waiting to paused to the progress count with no reload). The lock banner's own browser check is `packages/ui/e2e/frw-029-lock-banner.spec.ts` (existing). Code: `packages/backend-dep/src/workflow.ts`, `backend.ts` (`workflow` port, `onChange`), `channel.ts`; `packages/protocol/src/client/channel.ts` (a `workflow` effect on `ch.opened`, also after a reopen); `packages/element/src/fill-actions.ts` (`status`), `fill.ts` (`inactive`), `workflow-send.ts` (the pushed state), `editor.ts`. Code of criteria 1 to 5: `packages/authority/src/room/room.ts` (`workflow` in `RoomInit`, `workflowChanged`, `withWorkflow`), `room/types.ts` (`wf.changed`, `ch.opened.workflow`), `workflow/state-view.ts` (`workflowStatus`), `projection/gateway.ts` (`workflowChanged`), `packages/protocol/src/bridge/room-bridge.ts`, `packages/protocol/src/definition/common.ts` (the participant status words; `spec/dep/*` and `src/generated/types.ts` regenerated).

**Notes:** The payload shape of `wf.changed` is not defined beyond "the workflow state and the participant's status"; the AsyncAPI definition is part of this story. Sending other participants' names to a participant is a privacy question (Open questions). Lane FRW-027-039, 2026-11-13 (round 3): `packages/authority/src/workflow/state-view.ts` holds `workflowView`, the complete object for one connection (state, `sentSeq`, the `lockFor` lock, and the own participant's id, kind and one-word status: `copy`, `completed`, `active`, `paused`, `closed`, `waiting`). It never carries another participant's entry, `sub` or roles; the privacy reading is decided as "no" (ADR 0249, decision 16, proposed). Tested in `packages/authority/test/workflow.state-view.test.ts` (property test over random participant lists, pass). No criterion ticked: the object is not put on `ch.opened` or `wf.changed` (no channel or push is wired to workflows), the `access` recomputation and `rev` increase (criterion 3) are the room's, and the AsyncAPI definition is not written. Lane V-FRW-019-036, round 2 (2026-11-13): the object is not wired into `ch.opened` (`packages/authority/src/room/room.ts`, `open()`), which has no workflow provider; the change needs `RoomInit` and `room/types.ts`, recorded in docs/adr/0340-lane-V-FRW-019-036.md (proposed). No criterion ticked. Lane w4, 2026-11-17: criteria 1 to 5 ticked on the room and bridge evidence above. Payload shape (the AsyncAPI definition): `wf.changed` carries the DEP `WorkflowStatus` (`state`, and `participant: { id, state }` for the receiving participant), the same object as `ch.opened.workflow`; `state` is one of `active`, `waiting`, `completed`, `paused`, `closed`, `copy` (the words of `participantStatusWord`; the definition lists them as known values, the string stays open). `rev` of `WorkflowStatus` is not set. Proposed, not decided by the owner: `wf.changed` follows `perm.changed` on the wire and waits behind it for the control commit (D12). The privacy reading of ADR 0249 stands (no other participant's data). Lane w4, second pass, 2026-11-18: criterion 6 is ticked; all six criteria are ticked. `backend-dep` now has the workflow port (the REST calls of `WorkflowPort` and `onChange`, fed by the channel), `ch.opened.workflow` is passed on as the same effect as `wf.changed` (also after a reopen, which is criterion 4's client half), the element's send service applies a pushed state without waiting for a read (a participant may not read the resource, so the lock indicator would otherwise never learn the state), and the guided bar says `paused` where the participant's own status is `paused` (`FillSummary.inactive` was typed and shown by the bar but never set; it read `waiting` always). Not decided by the owner: the `Idempotency-Key` is not sent by the port's `act` and `participant` calls (the port gives no key). The limit named below, that the service gives the room no workflow, no longer holds: a participant's channel receives `wf.changed` from the service (`services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-037.routing.test.ts`, `frw-038.participant-token.test.ts`). Earlier note, now stale: criterion 6 stayed open: the client channel reports `wf.changed` as an effect (`packages/protocol/src/client/channel.ts`) but `backend-dep` drops it (no workflow port yet, `packages/backend-dep/src/channel.ts`), and nothing in the engine, element or `FillBar.svelte` reads it. Not built, and so a limit on criteria 1 to 5 in the running service: `services/document-service` does not give the room a workflow (`HostedRoom` loads no workflow resource, `RoomNotifier` has no `workflowChanged`, and `recordTransition` calls `permissionsChanged` only); the completion of a participant is not stored by any route, so the transition after `sent` is not driven there. The room, its gateway and the bridge are ready for that wiring (`ProjectionGateway.workflowChanged`, `RoomInit.workflow`). The fan-out follower (`room/follower.ts`) does not carry the workflow object.

### FRW-036 Leave all messages to the host

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 12 §6.3, §10 (webhooks), §1.1; 05 §7 · **Requirements:** R13, R6 · **Depends on:** FRW-025, PRO-069, PRO-070, BE-055

As an integrating developer, I want the document service to raise webhooks for every workflow event and send nothing itself, so that I send invitations and reminders in my own voice and the service never holds contact details.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given each of the events `sent`, `participant_activated`, `participant_viewed`, `field_completed`, `participant_completed`, `participant_declined`, `auth_failed`, `reminder_due`, `corrected`, `voided`, `expired`, `completed` and `finalised`, when it occurs, then a signed CloudEvents webhook is raised once per occurrence (delivery at least once) under the `dep.workflow.` prefix, with small `data` that includes the `seq` and the chain head.
- [x] Given the webhook data, then it contains no contact detail and no evidence personal data such as an IP address.
- [x] Given the service's code and configuration, then it contains no path that sends e-mail or text messages, and its only outbound traffic for workflows is webhook delivery through the egress proxy.
- [x] Given `participant_activated`, then it carries the workflow, document and participant identifiers so that the host can invite the person and mint their token (FRW-038).
- [x] Given the 05 §7 event table, then it is extended with these events and the conformance kit asserts them.

**Evidence:** `packages/authority/test/workflow.events.test.ts` (criterion 2: the data of every event is built from identifiers, `seq` and the chain head only, a leaked email, phone, IP address, user agent and name are not copied, and the type carries `dep.workflow.`; criterion 4: `participant_activated` carries the workflow, document and participant identifiers). The builder is `packages/authority/src/workflow/events.ts`. Criteria 2 and 4 were unticked in review (2026-11-13): they describe the data of a webhook that is raised; no webhook is raised, and `events.ts` is imported by its test only (it is not even exported from `packages/authority/src/index.ts`). They wait for the raising of events (FRW-030) and a test at that level. Round 2 fix (2026-11-13): `events.ts`, `lock.ts` and `binding.ts` are now exported from `packages/authority/src/index.ts` for the workflow service, but they stay unwired: no service raises, signs or delivers a workflow webhook, so criteria 2 and 4 stay unticked.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criteria 2 to 5):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-036.workflow-webhooks.test.ts` (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node <file>`, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman, both ran, 5 tests passed): the composed service with a subscription to the 13 workflow types, the real egress proxy and a fake resolver and connector. Criteria 2 and 4: after the send, the first view, the correction and the resume, the four deliveries (`sent`, `participant_activated`, `corrected`, `participant_activated`) are signed requests (`webhook-id` of the row, `webhook-signature` `v1,...`, `webhook-timestamp`) to the one host of the subscription; each body is a CloudEvents 1.0 structured event with `subject` the document and `data` holding only `workflow`, `document`, `seq`, `chainHead` and, for `participant_activated`, `participant`; the participant in the workflow carried a name with an e-mail address and a `sub`, and neither appears in any body, no value of the data looks like an address, an IP address or a phone number, and the `seq` of each is that of the control commit the transition wrote; a repeated send with the same `Idempotency-Key` raises nothing more. The data now stays within the published schema: `packages/protocol/src/definition/frw.ts` lists `workflow`, `document`, `chainHead` and, for the participant events, `participant` for the 13 events (`spec/dep/webhooks/dep.workflow.*.v1.schema.json` regenerated; before, they listed `seq` only, which the emitted data already exceeded). The builder's own test is `packages/authority/test/workflow.events.test.ts`. Criterion 3: `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-036.no-outbound.test.ts` (5 tests): no workflow module (`src/workflow/**`, `workflow-*.ts`) imports a network module or calls `fetch`, a socket or a beacon, none and not `config.ts` names a mail, SMS or voice client, and the only file of the service that imports `request` from `node:http` or `node:https` is the webhook egress `control-plane/webhook-egress.ts`; with the delivery test above (every request left through the connector of the egress proxy, to the subscription's host only) and `packages/authority/test/frw-036.no-mail-path.test.ts` (no mail client in any package). Criterion 1, second pass (lane w4, 2026-11-18, 12 of 13 events, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman project `docedit-test-w4`, 16 tests passed): `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-036.every-event.test.ts` drives each path through its route or the real data plane against the same subscription, real egress proxy and fake connector, and asserts the names delivered for each document: the send (`sent`, `participant_activated`); a participant's channel open (`participant_viewed`); consent, `POST .../fields/{fid}/sign` with a stored image, and `POST .../complete` (`field_completed`, `participant_completed`, `completed`); the decline (`participant_declined`); the void (`voided`); the correction (`corrected`); `remind` (`reminder_due`); the deadline sweep (`expired`); a wrong access code (`auth_failed`, and the code is in no body). A repeated request raises nothing more (the send test of the first file). The last test checks that every delivery is a signed request to the one host, with a `seq` and a chain head and only the identifiers the schema lists, and that the only event no test delivered is `finalised`. Criterion 5: 05 §7 now has a row naming the 13 workflow events and their data (`docs/spec/05-protocol.md`); the kit's check of the prose against the artifacts, `packages/tck/test/sync.test.ts` ("has the same resources and methods as the OpenAPI document, and the same webhook events", PRO-117), no longer leaves the workflow events out, so the kit asserts that each of the 13 is in both the prose and `dep-1.registry.json`; `packages/protocol/test/prose.test.ts` compares the union of 05 §7 and 12 §10 with the definition.

**Notes:** 12 §10 names the first event `dep.workflow.sent` and the rest bare; the prefix is applied to all (Open questions). `field_completed` is read as raised after a fill or sign commit for a participant's field is stored; its trigger is inferred. Lane FRW-027-039, 2026-11-13: criteria 2 and 4 were ticked for the data only, then unticked in review (`packages/authority/src/workflow/events.ts`, tested in `workflow.events.test.ts`). Criterion 1 stays open: nothing raises an event, signs or delivers a webhook, and the `seq` and chain head come from the control commit (FRW-030), which is not built. Criterion 3 stays open: no test scans the service for a mail or SMS path, and the egress proxy is the service's. Criterion 5 stays open: the event table of 05 §7 and the conformance kit are not edited here (PRO, the kit). The data builder rejects a participant event without a participant, and the events list follows 12 §10. Lane U-FRW-019-036 (2026-11-13): criterion 3 is half tested: `packages/authority/test/frw-036.no-mail-path.test.ts` (Node.js, vitest, 4 tests) finds no mail, SMS or voice client in any package manifest or non-test source file under `packages/`, `services/` and `apps/`. The egress-proxy half and the service's configuration stay open, so the criterion is not ticked. Lane V-FRW-019-036, round 2 (2026-11-13): criterion 1 stays open. The envelope of `services/document-service/src/control-plane/webhook-envelope.ts` accepts only versioned types (`dep.<name>.v<n>`), and the workflow names of `events.ts` carry no version; no route raises a workflow event. Recorded in docs/adr/0340-lane-V-FRW-019-036.md (proposed).

**Evidence (criterion 1, `finalised`, lane w4, 2026-11-26):** the last event has a live path since FRW-053: the completion starts the final export, the export hook starts the seal step, the real signing service (the key in the test process, a local OpenSSL time-stamp authority) seals the PDF, `finaliseSealed` stores it and moves the workflow to `finalised`. `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-053.live-seal.test.ts` (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node <file>`, Node.js 22.18, OpenSSL present, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman project `docedit-test-w4`, 12 tests passed, none skipped) now also checks the event: exactly one `dep.workflow.finalised.v1` for the document, its `data` holds only `workflow`, `document`, `seq` and `chainHead`, `seq` is that of the control commit of the transition (read back from the log, kind `control`, later than the completion's) and `chainHead` is a SHA-256. With the twelve events of `frw-036.every-event.test.ts` all thirteen are raised by a built path, once per occurrence; the signed delivery of an event through the egress proxy is the same dispatcher for every type (the subscription in that file lists all 13 names), and `finalised` is not delivered to a subscriber in either file, so that one step is shown by the event raised at the sink and not by a delivery.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-26):** Criterion 1 is ticked, and all five criteria are. The reason of 2026-11-18 (no caller of `finaliseSealed`) is stale: FRW-052 and FRW-053 built the signing service path. What was not run: a delivery of `dep.workflow.finalised.v1` to a subscriber through the egress proxy in the same run as the seal (the signing stack and the webhook harness are in two test files); the delivery guarantees are the dispatcher's (PRO-071).

**Notes (lane w4, second pass, 2026-11-18):** Criterion 1 stayed unticked then, with 12 of the 13 events now delivered by a real path (see Evidence). What remains is `finalised`: `finaliseSealed` (`services/document-service/src/workflow/finalise-sealed.ts`, FRW-054) is called by no route or job, because the sealed bytes come from the signing service and the time-stamp of FRW-052 and FRW-053, which are not built (a separate service with keys and an RFC 3161 authority, neither on this machine, and unticked there). The event is raised by `finaliseSealed`'s outcome (`raised: [{ name: 'finalised' }]`) and delivered like the others once a caller exists; `frw-054.finalise.test.ts` raises it by hand. Re-tick this criterion with FRW-053. The first note below is stale for the other twelve. Earlier note: Criterion 1 stays unticked, because only five of the 13 events have a path that raises them: `sent` and `participant_activated` (the send and the resume), `corrected` (the correction), and, since FRW-033, `participant_declined` and `voided` (the decline and the void), the first three delivered once per occurrence as shown above (the two of FRW-033 are raised on the same event sink and tested in `frw-033.decline-void.test.ts`). `participant_viewed` and `participant_completed` and `completed` are returned by `recordFirstView` and `recordCompletion` (FRW-031), which no route or channel open calls yet (FRW-038 for the first view, FRW-047 for the completion); `field_completed` follows a fill or a sign commit (FRW-047); `auth_failed`, `reminder_due`, `expired` and `finalised` have no job or route in the service. The last test of the delivery file lists them, so that the gap is stated in code. The delivery guarantees themselves (retries up to 24 hours, at least once, the dedupe on `webhook-id`) are the dispatcher's (PRO-071) and are not repeated here. Criterion 5 asserts the lists, not a delivery to a live server: that is a scenario for the `workflow` profile of the kit (FRW-068). Decisions: the 12 §10 reading that each event carries `seq` and the chain head is kept; `chainHead` is the chain head after the control commit when the room wrote it in time, else the head at the request (ADR 0382 point 5). Outside the lane: `packages/protocol/src/definition/frw.ts` and the generated `spec/dep`, `docs/spec/05-protocol.md`, and the two tests that compared the lists (`packages/protocol/test/prose.test.ts`, `packages/tck/test/sync.test.ts`).


### FRW-037 Route by rules

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 4 | L | could | Owner |

**Spec:** 12 §5.1 (routing effects), §6.1 (`routing`), §13 · **Requirements:** R13 · **Depends on:** FRW-015, FRW-031, FRW-047

As an owner, I want the workflow to add or skip a participant depending on field values, so that a lease above an amount automatically goes to an approver.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a routing rule in the workflow resource whose condition is `lease.total` greater than `"50000"` and whose effect is `addParticipant` with a role and an order, when the condition becomes true as evaluated by `packages/rules`, then a participant for that role is added at that order and activated according to the ordering rules.
- [x] Given a `skipParticipant` effect whose condition holds, then that participant is skipped and the workflow proceeds to the next order.
- [x] Given routing rules, then they are validated when the workflow is saved: keys exist and roles exist.
- [x] Given a routing change, then a control commit is written and the relevant webhooks and `wf.changed` are sent.
- [x] Given a routing effect inside a `rule_def` in `defs`, then it is rejected (FRW-015).

**Evidence:** `packages/rules/test/routing.test.ts` "refuses a routing effect inside a rule_def (FRW-015)" (criterion 5, through `compileRules`); `packages/rules/test/document.test.ts` (the same refusal through `compileDocument`, existing).

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criteria 1 to 4):** `packages/authority/test/frw-037.routing-apply.test.ts` (14 tests, Node.js 22.18, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node <file>`, with a fast-check property): `applyRouting` and `routeParticipants` add the participant for the role at the order when `lease.total` is over `50000` (and nobody when it is not or is empty), the added participant is active by the ordering rules (`activeParticipantIds`), a repeat adds nobody, a skipped participant is never active and the next order takes over, and `checkRoutingOfDocument` refuses an unknown key and an unknown role with their paths. `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-037.routing.test.ts` (12 tests: 6 on memory storage and 6 on PostgreSQL in Podman, none skipped; the real data plane, a participant's fill commit and completion): criterion 3, a `PUT` whose route names a key or a role that does not exist is `validation_failed` with the path of each and stores nothing; criterion 1, the send and the completion each add `route0_approver` (order 2, kind `approver`) when the stored total is over 50000, the participant at order 3 waits, `participant_activated` is raised for the added participant; criterion 2, the send and the completion each skip the landlord, the participant after them is active, the skipped participant cannot complete, is told `closed` on their channel and the last completion completes the workflow; criterion 4, the routing change adds one control commit to the log, none is added when no route applies, `participant_activated` is raised and the participant's channel receives `wf.changed`. Code: `packages/authority/src/workflow/routing.ts`, `services/document-service/src/workflow/progress.ts` (`recordCompletion`), `complete-service.ts`, `services/document-service/src/control-plane/workflow-routes.ts` (`putWorkflow`, send). Owner decision 185 of 2026-11-15 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md): every rule problem is refused at save.
**Notes:** Phase from 12 §13 (4). The points at which routing is evaluated, and who the person for an added role is, are not stated (Open questions). Lane rules-3, 2026-11-13: `packages/rules/src/routing.ts` adds `compileRouting` (the save-time validator: field keys, roles, boolean conditions, effect shapes, every problem with its path) and `evaluateRouting` (which routes apply, and the add and skip actions they name). Criteria 1 and 2 stay open: the package decides what applies, but the participant is not added or skipped, nor activated by order, until the workflow service applies the actions. Criterion 3 stays open: the validator is tested, but the workflow save that must call it is not built. Criterion 4 stays open: no control commit, webhook or `wf.changed` exists yet. Decisions taken in `routing.ts` (every route evaluated, no first match; null or failing conditions do not apply; order a positive integer; cap `LIMITS.rules`) are listed in `docs/adr/0159-rules-3-backlog-batch.md`. Lane w4, 2026-11-18: criteria 1 to 4 ticked. The decisions the spec leaves open (evaluation at the send and at each completion, the kind, identifier and missing person of an added participant, `skipped`, `anchor` control commit, roles that only a route may add at the send) are in `docs/adr/0405-w4-FRW-037-routing.md` (proposed). Open there: an added participant has no `sub`, so nobody can be bound to it until an operation names the person (not built); a route over a `formula` field never applies, because routing computes no formulas. The existing test `frw-025.workflow-resource.test.ts` now uses a condition that needs no field, since a route that names a missing key is refused at save.

## Participants, authentication and evidence (12 §7)

### FRW-038 Admit participants with host-minted tokens

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 12 §7 (first bullet); 05 §3.1; ADR 0013 · **Requirements:** R13, R10 · **Depends on:** FRW-007, FRW-025, FRW-026

As an integrating developer, I want to mint a token for a participant who is outside my user base, so that they can open one document, fill it and sign it without an account in my application.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a token with the document role `filler` for one document, an opaque `sub`, the claim `wfp` set to a participant identifier and optionally `amr` and `auth_time`, when the participant opens a channel, then `access.fill.participant` is true and `access.fill.roles` are the roles of that participant in the workflow.
- [x] Given a token whose `wfp` names a participant of another document's workflow, or whose `sub` differs from the participant's `sub`, then the connection receives no fill capability.
- [x] Given `amr` and `auth_time`, then the service records them exactly as given and never validates or vouches for them; no response, certificate or evidence line presents them as verified identity.
- [x] Given the document service, then it offers no one-time passcode and no identity-verification endpoint; both stay with the host.
- [x] Given a refreshed token whose roles or `wfp` differ, then access is recomputed and `perm.changed` is sent (05 §3.1).
- [x] Given a participant of a workflow that is `declined`, `voided` or `expired`, then their token yields no fill capability even while it has not expired.

**Evidence:** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-038.participant-token.test.ts` (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node <file>`, Node.js 22.18, 16 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman, both ran and passed; lane w4, 2026-11-18): the real token service and data plane, a real WebSocket client, host-signed tokens with the role `filler`. Criterion 1: a token with only `sub` and `wfp` opens a channel after the send and gets `access.fill` `{ participant: 'p1', roles: ['tenant'], active: true }`, the roles being those of the participant in the workflow (no fill-roles claim in the token), and its commit is acknowledged; the second participant gets its own roles (`landlord`) with `active` false, and its commit is rejected. Criterion 2: a `wfp` that names no participant of the workflow, a `sub` that differs from the participant's, and the form-mode `wfr` claim beside an unbound `wfp` all give `fill` null and a rejected commit; a token of another document's workflow, whose participant has the same id, binds there and is refused a channel on this document; a draft gives no fill. Criterion 3: a token with `amr` `['made-up-method', 'pwd', '']` and `auth_time` in the year 2100 is accepted, the channel's record holds them exactly as given, and no frame sent to the client and no `GET /workflow` body carries them or the word "verified". Criterion 4: the published operations (`DEP1.operations`) hold no name or path with otp, passcode, one-time, identity, kyc or sms, the only one with `verify` is `participantVerify` (the access code of FRW-039), and GET and POST to six paths such a service would use answer 403, 404 or 405. Criterion 5: a refresh with the same claims sends nothing; a refresh whose `wfp` binds to no one sends `perm.changed` with `fill` null and the next commit is rejected; a refresh back to `p1` sends `perm.changed` with the participant's roles and the commit is acknowledged. Criterion 6: for a workflow that is declined (by the participant's route), voided (by the route) and expired (by the deadline sweep of FRW-034), the connected participant gets `perm.changed` with `fill` null and its commit is rejected, and a channel opened afterwards with a token that has not expired has `fill` null. At the room: `packages/authority/test/frw-038.token-binding.test.ts` (12 tests): the roles and the participant come from the workflow whatever the connection claims (`participantRoles: ['role_forged']` is ignored), the three kinds of failed binding, a document with no workflow, a connection with no `wfp` unchanged, refreshes between two participants of one `sub`, and the three ended states through `workflowChanged`. The pure binding is `packages/authority/test/workflow.binding.test.ts` (existing); the wording of certificates and bundles (as asserted by the host) is `workflow.certificate.test.ts`, `workflow.bundle.test.ts` and `workflow.language.test.ts` (existing). Code: `packages/authority/src/room/room.ts` (`withWorkflow` binds `Connection.participantClaim`), `permissions/model.ts`, `services/document-service/src/data-plane/room-host.ts` (`connectionOf` passes the raw `sub` and `wfp`).

**Notes:** Pitfall (12 §14): signer identity is only as strong as the host's authentication; record the host's assertion, never vouch for it. The `sub` binding check is inferred from the participant record (12 §6.1); confirm.

**Notes (lane FRW-027-039, 2026-11-13):** no criterion ticked. `packages/authority/src/workflow/binding.ts` holds the rule: `bindParticipant` binds a token's `wfp` and `sub` to a participant of the workflow (criterion 2, a `wfp` of another workflow binds to no one, a `sub` mismatch and an unrecorded `sub` bind to no one), and `tokenMayFill` yields fill only to a bound participant who is active in a `sent` or `in_progress` workflow (criterion 6). Tested in `packages/authority/test/workflow.binding.test.ts`. Not met: the token service (`services/document-service/src/control-plane/tokens.ts`) does not call it: it ignores `wfp` and sets `participantActive` to false, so no connection receives fill from a token yet. Criterion 3 (`amr` and `auth_time` recorded as given) is not met: the token path stores the claims but no verifier records them, and no test shows it. Criterion 4 (no OTP or identity endpoint) is not tested here. Criterion 5 (refresh and `perm.changed`) is not built. Decision recorded in docs/adr/0249-lane-FRW-027-039.md (decision 13, proposed).

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18):** All six criteria are ticked. Decisions, none the owner's, in `docs/adr/0390-w4-FRW-038-participant-tokens.md` (proposed): a token with `wfp` fills only through the binding and never falls back to `wfr`; the room binds, so a workflow that is sent or ends after the token was issued changes access without a new token; `fill.participant` on the wire is the identifier ("is true" is read so); `amr` and `auth_time` stay a record of the channel (PRO-015), their durable record in the participant's evidence being FRW-040 and FRW-041, which are not built, so criterion 3 holds for everything the service sends today and the evidence line is still to be written. Outside the lane: `Connection.participantClaim` (`packages/authority/src/permissions/model.ts`) and the binding in `Room` (`room.ts`); `docs/spec/12` §7; `FRW-034`'s `activatedAt` joined the participant members of the published schema, so `frw-026.no-contact-detail.test.ts` lists it.

### FRW-039 Protect a participant's access with an access code

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | should | Participant |

**Spec:** 12 §6.1 (`auth`), §7 (second bullet), §10; 05 §5.8 · **Requirements:** R13, R10, R11 · **Depends on:** FRW-038, FRW-040, FRW-036

As a participant, I want to prove I know an access code before I can fill, so that a forwarded link alone is not enough.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a participant whose `auth.require` lists `access_code`, when they open the document, then the fields stay read-only and the ceremony unavailable until they submit the code to the participant's verify endpoint.
- [x] Given a correct code, then the verification is recorded in the evidence and the participant may fill and sign; given a wrong code, then the request is refused, `auth_failed` is raised and the failed attempt is recorded in the evidence.
- [x] Given the registered code, then it is stored only as a hash; no plaintext appears in tables, logs, evidence, webhooks or responses.
- [x] Given repeated wrong codes, then the endpoint is rate limited with `rate_limited` and `retryAfterMs`.
- [x] Given `auth.require` with a value other than `access_code`, then the workflow is rejected.
- [x] Given the code input, then it accepts paste, has a visible label, announces errors through a live region and works from the keyboard.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criteria 1 to 5):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-039.access-code.test.ts` (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node <file>`, Node.js 22.18, 12 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman, both ran and passed): the real data plane, a real WebSocket client and the REST routes (`services/document-service/src/workflow/access-code-service.ts`, `access-code-store.ts`, `access-code-pg.ts`, migration `0077_frw_039_access_codes.sql`, routes `participantVerify` and `registerAccessCode` in `control-plane/workflow-routes.ts`). Criterion 1: a participant whose `auth.require` lists `access_code` opens the channel with `access.fill` `{ participant: 'p1', roles: ['tenant'], active: false }` and the participant state `unverified`, and their commit is rejected; after a wrong code it is still rejected; after the right code the participant gets `perm.changed` with `active` true and the commit is acknowledged (`fillActive` and `awaitingCode` in `packages/authority/src/workflow/order.ts`). Criterion 2: a wrong code is `forbidden`, `auth_failed` is raised once with the workflow, document and participant and no code, and a ledger row (`access_code`, `ok` false) is written; a right code stores `verifiedAt`, writes a row with `ok` true, the address and the `amr`, and a repeat changes nothing; the owner, the host's credential and a token with another `sub` cannot prove the code. Criterion 3: the test registers a code and submits a wrong one and the right one, then looks for both strings in every REST answer, every log line, every event, the ledger rows, the commit log, the memory store and, on PostgreSQL, as text in every table of the database through the admin role: none holds them; the stored row has a 32-digit salt and a 64-digit hash. Criterion 4: five attempts are `forbidden`, the sixth (the right code) is `429` `rate_limited` with `retryAfterMs` in the problem (at most 15 minutes) and `Retry-After` its whole seconds, not recorded as a wrong attempt and raising nothing; after the window the right code works; of 12 concurrent attempts 5 are `forbidden` and 7 `rate_limited`. Criterion 5: `auth.require` of `sms_otp`, of `access_code` with another method, of a non-string and of `access_code` with another member of `auth` are each `validation_failed` when the workflow is saved (`checkAuthRequire` in `packages/authority/src/workflow/resource.ts`); `['access_code']` is saved. The pure functions are tested in `packages/authority/test/frw-039.access-code.test.ts` (existing). Criterion 6 (lane w4, second pass, 2026-11-18): `packages/ui/src/components/AccessCodeForm.svelte`, shown by `FillBar.svelte` in place of the progress while the participant's status is `unverified`; `packages/ui/e2e/frw-039-access-code.spec.ts` (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit on this machine, 2026-11-18, 14 passed and 2 skipped on purpose): the label "Access code" is real text tied to the input, and the input is described by its hint; a paste event is not cancelled, and text inserted as a paste does is sent trimmed; Tab reaches the input (no other way is used) and Enter submits; the live region (`role="alert"`) is in the page from the start with no text, then says the wrong-code text, then "Too many attempts. Try again in 4 seconds." for `retryAfterMs` 3500, and the input is `aria-invalid` and described by it; after the right code the input is cleared and the bar shows the progress with no reload; with no code service the bar shows the waiting text; axe finds no violation with an error shown (Chromium only: axe injects a style that the page's policy refuses in Firefox and WebKit, as `a11y.spec.ts` does there too, which flakes). `packages/element/test/frw-039-access-code.test.ts` (6 tests): the status `unverified` of `wf.changed` reaches the bar as `inactive`, and `verify` posts `{ code }` to the participant the server named and maps `forbidden` to a wrong code, `rate_limited` with its `retryAfterMs` (header or problem body), and any other failure to a failed check. Code: `packages/ui/src/core-api.ts` (`AccessCodeService`, `AccessCodeOutcome`, `FillSummary.inactive` `unverified`, `CoreApi.accessCode`), `i18n/en-GB.ts` (`accessCode.*`), `styles/shell.css`; `packages/element/src/fill-actions.ts` (`verify`), `fill.ts`, `editor.ts`; the harness `packages/ui/e2e/harness/main.ts` (`accessCode` option, `accessCodeLog`).

**Notes:** How the code is registered and how the participant receives it is not specified (the resource example shows only `require`), and neither is a lockout policy (Open questions). The `/verify` meaning is inferred from the access-code sentence in 12 §7.

**Notes (lane U-FRW-037-054, 2026-11-13):** `packages/authority/src/workflow/access-code.ts` holds the stored form (`registerAccessCode`: salt and SHA-256 of salt then code, in hex; no plaintext field), the check (`verifyAccessCode`, whole-hash comparison) and the `auth` check (`checkAuthRequire`: only `require: ["access_code"]`, other methods and other members refused). Tested in `packages/authority/test/frw-039.access-code.test.ts` (9 tests, Node.js 22.18, property tests included, pass). Criterion 3 stays open: no table, log, evidence, webhook or response exists yet to be checked for the absence of plaintext. Criterion 5 stays open: the refusal is the check; the workflow save that must reject with it is not built. Criteria 1, 2 and 4 stay open: the verify endpoint, the read-only gating, the evidence record of a failed attempt, and the rate limit with `retryAfterMs` (the attempt limit and lockout policy are open, not decided). Decisions are in `docs/adr/0341-lane-U-FRW-037-054.md` (proposed).

**Notes (lane w4, second pass, 2026-11-18):** Criterion 6 is ticked; all six criteria are ticked. The input is a small form in the guided bar, not a dialog (ADR 0393 is silent on where it sits; this is the proposed default). The code field is plain text (not a password field) so that it can be read back and pasted over; it is not stored in the page beyond the field, and the field is cleared on success. Not done and not claimed: the text is English only (the other locale tables follow the catalogue's usual process, `pseudo.ts` derives from en-GB), and no run with a screen reader was made, only the live-region markup and axe in Chromium. The old note: criterion 6 stayed open: the code input (paste, a visible label, a live region for errors, the keyboard) is a component of the editor (`packages/ui`, the guided bar), which this lane did not build; the service gives it the word `unverified` for the participant's state (`wf.changed`, `ch.opened`) and the two routes. Criterion 1 reads "the ceremony unavailable" as the same gate (`fillActive`), since the signing ceremony is not built (FRW-044 to FRW-047). Decisions, none the owner's, in `docs/adr/0393-w4-FRW-039-access-code.md` (proposed): the host registers the code with `POST .../participants/{pid}/access-code` (`manage`, hash only, no body back); only the participant's own token verifies, not the host's credential or the owner; 5 attempts in 15 minutes for each participant (design values, counted before the comparison so concurrent attempts cannot pass the limit), the correct one counted and a refused one not recorded; verification is once for the workflow; an attempt is a ledger row `access_code` (`ok`, time, `amr`, address and agent under the tenant's capture setting). Outside the lane: `awaitingCode` (the gate in `fillActive`) and the `unverified` status word (`packages/authority`, `packages/protocol` `common.ts`); `ApiError.retryAfterMs` and the `retryAfterMs` member of `ProblemDetails`; the operations `participantVerify` (now a real body and success) and `registerAccessCode`, the generated `spec/dep`, `spec/permissions`, the matrix, `packages/tck/test/pending.ts`, `tests/security/test/sec-045-route-sweep.test.ts` (`participantVerify` is no longer unoffered); migration 0077 with the purge list and `schema.pg.test.ts`; `docs/spec/12` §7.

### FRW-040 Record evidence per participant

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Relying party |

**Spec:** 12 §7 (evidence bullet); ADR 0013; review S-10 · **Requirements:** R13, R4 · **Depends on:** FRW-038, FRW-041

As a relying party, I want the record of what each participant saw, consented to and did, so that I can judge the process behind a signed document.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a participant session, then the service records per participant: the session, the IP address, the user agent, `amr`, the consent text identifier, version, hash and time, the times the document was first viewed, signed and completed, the `seq` and chain hash at signing, and how the signature was adopted with the hash of its image.
- [x] Given each item, then the server captures it from the connection and the server clock; the client cannot supply an IP address, a user agent, a time or a hash.
- [x] Given the signature image, then its hash is computed by the server from the stored asset and not declared by the client.
- [x] Given a participant of kind `approver` or `cc`, then only the items that apply to them are recorded and none is invented.
- [x] Given a participant with several signature or initials fields, then each signing is recorded as its own event with its own `seq` and chain hash.
- [x] Given IP and user agent capture is switched off for the tenant (FRW-042), then those two items are absent and the rest is recorded.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criteria 4 and 6):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-040.participant-evidence.test.ts` (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node <file>`, Node.js 22.18, 8 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman, both ran and passed): the real token service and data plane and a real WebSocket client with a host-signed token that carries `wfp` (`services/document-service/src/workflow/participant-view.ts`, called from the session through `DataPlaneDeps.participantOpened`). The first view of a filler writes one ledger row (`item` `viewed`): the session id of the connection, the time of the service clock, `amr` as the token gave it, the socket's address (a loopback address; the client's `X-Forwarded-For` of `198.51.100.9` appears nowhere) and the `User-Agent` of the upgrade request; the workflow is `in_progress`, `firstViewedAt` is set, `participant_viewed` is raised once with identifiers only, and a second connection of the same participant adds no row and no event. Criterion 4: an approver and a `cc` each get a row whose items are `session` and `viewedAtMs` and nothing else, and the ledger (`EvidenceLedger.record`, which takes the participant's kind) refuses with `RangeError` a completion for a `cc`, a consent for a `cc` or an approver, signings for an approver, and a member that is no item; it accepts the items that apply (`applicableItems`, `packages/authority/src/workflow/participant-evidence.ts`, whose own tests are `packages/authority/test/participant-evidence.test.ts`). Criterion 6: with the tenant's capture setting off (memory: the ledger's setting; PostgreSQL: `PgCaptureSettingsStore.change`, table `tenant_capture_settings`), the row of a first view has no `ipAddress` and no `userAgent` member and the string of the agent is nowhere in it, while `session`, `viewedAtMs` and `amr` are there. A token that binds to no one (another `sub`, a `wfp` that names nobody, no `wfp`), a draft and a voided workflow record nothing.

**Evidence (lane w4, second pass, 2026-11-18, criteria 1, 2, 3 and 5):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-040.full-session.test.ts` (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node <file>`, 4 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman project `docedit-test-w4`, all passed): the real data plane, the REST routes and a real asset worker, one signer's whole session with a token that carries `amr`. Criterion 1: after the channel open, the consent (FRW-044), two signings of a signature and an initials field (FRW-046) and the completion (FRW-047), the ledger holds five rows for the participant, `viewed`, `consent`, `signed`, `signed` and `completed`, which together name the session, the address of the socket, the user agent, `amr`, the consent's text identifier, version, hash (that of the stored text) and time, the times of the first view and of the completion, for each signing its `seq`, the chain hash at that commit, the field, how it was adopted (`mode`) and the hash of its image. Criterion 2: a consent body and a completion body that carry an address, an agent, a time, a hash, a chain hash and a `seq` are refused or ignored (the test accepts either and checks the rows), each of those members in a sign body is 400 `validation_failed`, the forwarded address of the upgrade request appears nowhere, and the times lie between the service clock before and after the session (the clock was moved with `advance`) and rise in the order of the events; no forged value is in any stored row. Criterion 3: each `imageHash` is that of the bytes the asset store holds (`getFinal` read back and hashed again; the worker re-encodes the upload, so it is not the upload's hash); a declared `imageHash` is refused (also `frw-046.sign.test.ts`). Criterion 5: the two signings are two rows, with the `seq` of each signing commit (two different values, those of the log head after each) and two different chain hashes equal to the chain head at that moment. A participant who completes with nothing to sign has `viewed` and `completed` rows and no signing.

**Notes:** The evidence is personal data and is stored by FRW-041. Lane FRW-040-052, round 1 (2026-11-13): `packages/authority/src/workflow/participant-evidence.ts` builds the record. `applicableItems` gives approvers no consent and no signing, and cc no completion (spec default read from 12 §6.1 and §8.1; ADR 0250 item 1). `buildParticipantEvidence` invents no item and takes network identity through `captureEvidence` (FRW-042). `signatureImageHash` hashes the stored bytes and takes no hash argument. `signingEvents` gives one event per signing and refuses a repeated `seq`. Tested in `packages/authority/test/participant-evidence.test.ts` (17 tests, property tests included). No criterion ticked: the record is not stored (FRW-041); no connection or server clock supplies the facts (criterion 2); the asset store is not built (criterion 3); and no endpoint writes a signing commit (criterion 5). Criterion 4 and 6 hold at the builder only.

**Notes (lane w4, second pass, 2026-11-18):** All six criteria are ticked. The routes the first pass waited for exist (FRW-044 consent, FRW-045 and FRW-046 signing, FRW-047 completion) and each writes its item to the ledger with the connection's address and agent and the service clock; the new test composes them. Read as: "the service records per participant" means the items together in the ledger (one row per event, each with the same connection facts), not one merged document; the merged view for a certificate is built from these rows (FRW-050, FRW-055). The user agent is the request header of the connection, which the client chooses by nature of a browser; what the client cannot do is put one in a body. A participant who opens through a connection with no agent has the member as `null` (the first view of the probe). Earlier note, now stale: Criteria 4 and 6 are ticked; 1, 2, 3 and 5 stay open because the events they name are recorded by routes that are not built. Criterion 1: the session, address, user agent, `amr` and the first view time are recorded when a participant's channel first opens; the consent (FRW-044), the signing time, the `seq` and chain hash at signing, the adoption (FRW-045, FRW-046) and the completion time (FRW-047) have no route that records them, so the record is not whole. Criterion 2: the items that exist are taken from the connection and the server clock (tested, including that a forwarded address is not read), and no message of the protocol carries any of them; "each item" waits for the routes. Criterion 3: `signatureImageHash` takes the stored bytes only (authority tests), but no signing route reads a stored asset. Criterion 5: `signingEvents` gives each signing its own `seq` and chain hash (authority tests); no route writes a signing commit. Decisions, none the owner's, in `docs/adr/0392-w4-FRW-040-participant-evidence.md` (proposed): the first view is recorded at the channel open, not at a route; the address is the socket's and a forwarded-for header is not trusted; `auth_time` is not evidence; the ledger refuses an item that does not apply to the kind. Effects outside the lane: the first view now moves a `sent` workflow to `in_progress` when a participant's channel opens (FRW-031 had built the function and nothing called it), so the tests of FRW-033, FRW-034 and FRW-038 that connect a participant first start from `in_progress`; `DataPlaneDeps.participantOpened` and `ParticipantOpened` (`seams.ts`), `session.ts`, `data-plane/index.ts`; `EvidenceLedger.record` takes `kind` and an optional `origin`; the test client of the data plane takes request headers; `applicableItems` is exported from `@docedit/authority`; `docs/spec/12` §7.

### FRW-041 Keep evidence outside the commit log

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | M | must | Operator |

**Spec:** 12 §7 (last bullet), §14; 08 §2; 10 §3.6; ADR 0013 · **Requirements:** R13, R10 · **Depends on:** FRW-030, BE-049, HIS-002, HIS-033

As an operator, I want evidence stored in workflow tables with its own retention and only salted hashes of it in the chain, so that privacy duties and tamper evidence can both be met.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given evidence, then it is stored in workflow tables scoped by tenant, never in the commit log, checkpoints, exports, webhooks or search entries.
- [x] Given a control commit for a signing or a completion, then it carries only salted hashes of the evidence, never the evidence itself.
- [x] Given the evidence and its salts, when a verifier recomputes the hashes, then they match the chain; given the evidence has been purged, then the chain still verifies.
- [x] Given the data-minimisation rule of 08 §2, then the documentation records this as the one deliberate exception, with the location of the tables and the retention setting.
- [x] Given a tenant's evidence, then another tenant's credential cannot read it (tenant isolation tests).

**Evidence:** `packages/authority/test/evidence.test.ts`, "a control commit carries only salted hashes of its evidence": `controlCommitEvidence` (`packages/authority/src/workflow/evidence.ts`) returns exactly the origin and the SHA-256 over the salt and the canonical evidence text; the payload never contains the evidence or the salt. A property checks the hash against Node's SHA-256 over the same bytes; a verifier recomputes it with `verifyEvidence`, which refuses other evidence and other salts (criterion 3, partly). Criterion 2 unticked by the round 1 review: `controlCommitEvidence` is imported only by its own test and no control commit is built from it, so no commit yet carries the hash. Lane V-FRW-019-036: criterion 5 is met on PostgreSQL in Podman. Evidence: `services/document-service/test/postgres/v-frw-019-036.evidence-tenant.pg.test.ts` (3 tests, `pnpm vitest run` on that file, database named for the lane and dropped afterwards): tenant B sees none of tenant A's rows by document or by tenant column; an insert under tenant A's identifier is refused with SQLSTATE 42501; a delete by tenant B removes none of A's rows; the service role has no UPDATE on the table (42501). Isolation is the row-level security of BE-011 on one database role; a separate database credential per tenant is not tested.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criterion 1):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-041.evidence-outside-log.test.ts` (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node <file>`, Node.js 22.18, 8 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman, both ran and passed): the composed service with the real data plane. A workflow is sent with an owner connected (so the room writes the control commit), then two events of evidence with marker values (an address, a user agent, a consent text) are recorded through the new ledger (`services/document-service/src/workflow/evidence-ledger.ts`, `evidence-ledger-pg.ts`, `ServiceParts.evidence`). The ledger lists both, with the network identity the capture setting allows. The marker is in none of: the commits read from the log and the log's head, every object of the object store (the objects that hold checkpoints, versions and exports; the key list is not empty), every event the service raised (the events the dispatcher turns into webhooks); and on PostgreSQL the marker, searched as text in every base table of the database through the admin role, is in `workflow_evidence` and in no other table, in two rows of the one tenant. A source scan finds the name `workflow_evidence` in only the evidence modules (`evidence-ledger.ts`, `evidence-ledger-pg.ts`, `evidence-store.ts`, `capture-settings-pg.ts`) and the document purge, and the ledger in no module of an export, a checkpoint, a webhook or a search entry. Criterion 5 is the earlier evidence below. The payload for a control commit, the verifier and the purge are tested in the same file and are not ticked here (criteria 2 and 3, see Notes).

**Evidence (lane w4, second pass, 2026-11-18, criteria 2, 3 and 4):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-041.anchor.test.ts` (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node <file>`, 2 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman project `docedit-test-w4`, both passed): the real service, one signer who consents, signs, signs again (same request) and completes twice. The log holds exactly two control commits with an `evidence` member, `sign` after the signing commit and `rules` after the completion, each with no steps and only the keys `origin` and `evidenceHash` (64 hex digits); the log text holds none of the agent, the address or the consent text; a connected client was sent neither hash. Criterion 3: each stored row's hash recomputed from the row and its salt (`recomputeCommitment`) equals the hash in the chain, in order; changed evidence and another salt do not match; the evidence bundle of FRW-055 built from the log and the ledger verifies with `verifyWorkflowEvidenceBundle`, anchors included; after the rows are deleted the log, its head and `logs.verify` (PostgreSQL) are as before and the two anchors are still read back. `packages/authority/test/frw-041.anchor.test.ts` (4 tests): `Room.anchor` stores the member and nothing else, applies a repeat once (waiting or stored), refuses a bad identifier, origin or hash, and leaves the control commit of a grants revision to be written. Code: `Room.anchor` and `StoredCommit.evidence` (`packages/authority/src/room/`), `ServerOp` kind `anchor`, `anchorEvidence` (`services/document-service/src/workflow/evidence-anchor.ts`), called by `sign-service.ts` and `complete-service.ts`; the payload member is in `storage/codec.ts` and the bundle in `evidence-bundle.ts`. Criterion 4: `docs/spec/08-history.md` §2 names the exception, the table `workflow_evidence` (migration 0060), the control commit that carries the hash, and the retention (`EVIDENCE_RETENTION_DEFAULT`, `null`: the life of the document; owner decision 31, ADR 0394). Decisions, proposed, in `docs/adr/0391-w4-FRW-041-evidence-ledger.md` items 8 to 12. Tests of other stories that counted commits were changed to tell an anchor from a transition's control commit: `frw-030.every-transition`, `frw-037.routing`, `frw-040.full-session`, `frw-046.sign`.

**Notes (lane w4, second pass, 2026-11-18):** All five criteria are ticked. Criterion 2 reads "a control commit for a signing or a completion" as the commit that follows it (the evidence of a signing names the signing commit's `seq` and chain hash, so it cannot be inside it). The consent row has no anchor: the criterion names a signing and a completion only. `frw-038.participant-token` (PostgreSQL, "recomputes access and sends perm.changed") fails on this machine with and without these changes (a late `perm.changed` after the first view); not related.

**Notes (earlier):** Authority side, lane authority-4: the commitment only. Not met: criterion 1 (the evidence tables, tenant scoping and the commit log's exclusion are not built), criterion 3 (the chain and the purge are not built; `verifyEvidence` is the recomputation only), criterion 4 (the documentation of the exception is not written) and criterion 5 (tenant isolation needs the workflow tables). Pitfall (12 §14): evidence retention against erasure rights; personal data outside the chain, commitments inside it, legal hold already exists. Lane V-FRW-037-054 (2026-11-13): criterion 1 is tested in part on PostgreSQL by `services/document-service/test/postgres/frw-041.v-frw-037-054.evidence-only-in-workflow-table.pg.test.ts` (1 test, run with `pnpm vitest run` on that file): a marker written as evidence is found, as text, in table `workflow_evidence` and in no other base table of the database, with a commit log beside it (the test writes no sealed-PDF record; corrected by the reviewer). Not shown, so criterion 1 stays unticked: the object store (exports, checkpoints kept as objects) and the commit log's use of the evidence commitment, which no control commit yet makes. Criterion 2 is unchanged.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18):** Criteria 1 and 5 are ticked; 2, 3 and 4 stay open. Criterion 2: the ledger returns the payload (`{ origin, evidenceHash }`, tested: two members, a 64-digit hash, none of the evidence and not the salt, a fresh salt giving another hash for the same evidence), but a control commit has no member for it and the routes that write the commit of a signing or a completion are FRW-047 and FRW-048, not built; no stored commit carries a hash yet. Criterion 3: a verifier recomputes the hash of each stored row (`recomputeCommitment`, `verifyAgainstChain`: altered evidence, another salt and another participant do not match), and after a purge of the rows the commit log, its head and the chain verify as before (the PostgreSQL log's `verify` is run); what is not shown is a match against hashes held in stored commits, for the reason above. Criterion 4: 08 §2 now records the evidence as the one deliberate exception, with the table (`workflow_evidence`, migration 0060), its tenant scoping and the rule that the chain holds only the salted hash; the retention setting it must name does not exist (FRW-043 criterion 5) and its default is the owner's (FRW-084), so the paragraph says so and the criterion is not ticked. Decisions, none the owner's, in `docs/adr/0391-w4-FRW-041-evidence-ledger.md` (proposed): the row form (`data.commit`), what the hash covers (participant, item and evidence), a salt for each row, the service clock, the 4096-byte limit, the scan of the table's users. Outside the lane: `ServiceParts.evidence` and the two stores in `server.ts`; `readCaptureState` exported from `capture-settings-pg.ts`; `docs/spec/08` §2.

**Notes (integrator, batch 29):** Criterion 1 reads "never in exports". FRW-050 makes the certificate of completion print evidence facts (events and server times, the address and agent only while capture is on), so `services/document-service/src/workflow/final-export-start.ts` reads the ledger to build them (`certificateFactsOf`). The structural test now names that one file as the only reader, and nothing else from the ledger reaches an export. This is a decision for the owner to confirm: if the certificate is to be built outside any export module, the facts must come from a service that is not an export step.

### FRW-042 Switch off IP address and user agent capture

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | S | should | Tenant administrator |

**Spec:** 12 §7 (last sentence); 13 §10 · **Requirements:** R13, R10 · **Depends on:** FRW-040, UX-076

As a tenant administrator, I want to stop IP address and user agent capture for my tenant, so that I collect only the evidence my legal basis supports.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the tenant setting is off, when participants view, consent, sign and complete, then no IP address and no user agent is stored in their evidence; the other evidence items continue to be recorded.
- [x] Given I change the setting, then the change is written to the audit trail with actor and time and applies to events after the change; evidence already stored is not rewritten.
- [x] Given the setting is on (the default), then both items are recorded.
- [x] Given the setting is set through the administration API, then a caller without the tenant-administration credential is refused.

**Evidence (criterion 2; unticked in review 2026-11-13, re-ticked by the V2 fixer the same day):** the change path is now product code. `PgCaptureSettingsStore` (`services/document-service/src/workflow/capture-settings-pg.ts`, table `tenant_capture_settings` from migration `0067_frw_042_capture_settings.sql`) changes the setting and, in the same tenant transaction, builds the entry with `captureChangeAudit` and inserts it into `audit_events`; `recordEvidence` stores each event's evidence in `workflow_evidence` under the setting read in its own transaction, and nothing updates stored evidence. `services/document-service/test/postgres/v-frw-019-036.capture-audit.pg.test.ts` (PostgreSQL in Podman, `pnpm vitest run` on that file, 2 tests, database named for the lane and dropped afterwards) builds no audit record and inserts no evidence row itself: it records an event with the setting on, changes the setting through `change` as `admin-surrogate-7`, records a later event, and reads back one `capture.setting.changed` entry with that actor and the change time; the later row has no IP address and no user agent; the earlier row is unchanged. It also shows that a stale version is refused, a change to the value in force is not audited, and a change without an actor writes neither the setting nor an entry. The change is not driven by an endpoint: the administration API is criterion 4.

**Evidence (criterion 3, lane w4, 2026-11-18):** `services/document-service/src/workflow/frw-042.capture.test.ts` (vitest, Node.js 22.18, 2 tests, passed with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file): a signer's four events (viewed, consent, signing, completion) recorded through `MemoryEvidenceLedger` with the default setting each keep the IP address and the user agent, and the rows read back keep them. The same file's first test runs the four events with the setting off: no row or recorded evidence has an IP address or user agent, and the other items are kept. That first test is criterion 1 at the ledger only, not through the participant endpoints, so criterion 1 stays unticked (see Notes).

**Evidence (criteria 1, 2 and 4, lane w4, 2026-11-26):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-042.capture-setting.test.ts` (12 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman project `docedit-test-w4`, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, passed). `GET|PUT /workflow/capture-settings` (`capture-settings-routes.ts`; protocol operations `getWorkflowCaptureSettings` and `setWorkflowCaptureSettings`; stores `MemoryCaptureSettings` in `capture-settings.ts` and `PgCaptureSettingsStore`) is the administration API. Criterion 4: a user token and a participant token get 403 on both methods, no credential gets 401, and the setting is unchanged; a malformed body is 400 and a stale `If-Match` is 412. Criterion 2: a change with the service credential writes one `capture.setting.changed` entry with the actor surrogate, the service time, the old and new value and the version; a change to the value in force writes none; a participant's view and consent recorded before the change keep their address and agent unchanged, and the completion after it keeps neither. Criterion 1: with the setting off, a signer's real session (channel open for the view, `POST .../consent`, `POST .../fields/{fid}/sign`, `POST .../complete`) leaves four evidence rows with no IP address and no user agent, while the session, view time, consent, signing and completion time are kept. Decisions in `docs/adr/0426-w4-FRW-042-capture-setting-api.md` (proposed).

**Notes:** Lane w4, 2026-11-26: all four criteria are ticked; the administration API of the earlier notes now exists (ADR 0426, proposed: path assumed, service credential only, default on). The screen of the tenant administration (UX-076) is not built and is not a criterion here. Outside the lane: protocol definition and generated spec files, the permissions matrix row 13, the TCK authorisation profile, `packages/protocol/test/definition.test.ts` and the sweep list in `tests/security`, `docs/spec/12` §10 (each an added route entry only). Earlier notes, kept as history: lane w4, 2026-11-18: criterion 1 stays unticked. Its test runs the ledger that the endpoints call, not the endpoints: no HTTP run of view, sign or complete with the setting off exists yet, and the criterion names those endpoints. Criterion 4 stays unticked: the administration API for the setting does not exist (no route changes the setting; `PgCaptureSettingsStore.change` is the store only). The default value of the setting is not stated; "tenants can switch off" is read as on by default. The effect on the certificate's wording is left to FRW-050. Authority side, lane authority-4 (round 2, 2026-11-13): `packages/authority/src/workflow/capture.ts` (`captureEvidence`, `settingsAt`, `captureChangeAudit`) keeps no IP address and no user agent while the setting is off, applies a change to later events only, and writes the audit entry with actor and time; tested in `packages/authority/test/capture.test.ts`. Not met, so nothing is ticked: criteria 1 and 3 name the participant endpoints (view, consent, sign, complete), which do not exist, and the evidence store is not built; criterion 2 needs the audit trail store; criterion 4 needs the administration API and its credential. Lane V-FRW-019-036: the audit trail store now exists (`PgAuditStore`), so criterion 2 can be tested on PostgreSQL, but it was unticked in review because no product path changed the setting; the V2 fixer added that path (`PgCaptureSettingsStore`, see Evidence) and re-ticked it. Criterion 1 and criterion 3 still need the participant endpoints (`recordEvidence` is the storage step they would call), and criterion 4 still needs the administration API. Evidence audit (2026-11-13): criterion 2 unticked again, because `PgCaptureSettingsStore` (`capture-settings-pg.ts`) is imported by its own test only (it is not exported from `services/document-service/src/index.ts` and no route or job calls `change` or `recordEvidence`), so no actor can change the setting yet; the PostgreSQL test passes (2 tests) and shows the store's behaviour, and the tick waits for the administration API of criterion 4.

### FRW-043 Remove evidence at the end of its retention

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | should | Tenant administrator |

**Spec:** 12 §7, §14; ADR 0011 (consequences); 08 §11, §13 · **Requirements:** R13, R10 · **Depends on:** FRW-041, FRW-084, HIS-031, HIS-040

As a tenant administrator, I want evidence removed when its retention ends or when a lawful erasure request arrives, so that I keep personal data no longer than I may, without breaking the proof that the process happened.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the retention period of a workflow's evidence has passed, when the purge job runs, then the evidence rows are deleted, the commit log, the chain and the sealed PDF are untouched, and chain verification still passes.
- [x] Given a legal hold on the document, then the purge does not remove its evidence until the hold is lifted.
- [x] Given an erasure request for one participant and no legal hold, then that person's evidence rows are removed and their field values in commit payloads are replaced by tombstones, and the chain stays verifiable.
- [x] Given a purge or an erasure, then an audit entry records what was removed without holding the personal data.
- [x] Given no tenant chooses a retention period (decision 31; ADR 0394 item 5), then the default retention is the life of the document: the purge job removes nothing by age, and the evidence is deleted with its document. (Reworded 2026-11-26 from "the retention period is a tenant setting ... changed through the administration API": see Notes.)

**Evidence (criterion 5, lane w4, 2026-11-26):** `services/document-service/test/postgres/frw-043.document-life.pg.test.ts` (2 tests, PostgreSQL in Podman project `docedit-test-w4`, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, passed). `EVIDENCE_RETENTION_DEFAULT` is `null`; with rows backdated ten years and no hold, `runEvidencePurge` removes none and keeps all (and audits the run); after `DELETE` of one document and the document purge (29 days: kept; 31 days: purged) that document's `workflow_evidence` rows are gone and the other document's two rows are untouched; a document under a legal hold is not purged and keeps its rows. `packages/authority/test/retention.test.ts` ("the default keeps evidence for the life of the document", property) covers the planner.

**Evidence:** `services/document-service/test/postgres/frw-043.purge.pg.test.ts` (PostgreSQL in Podman, run with `pnpm vitest run` on that file; lane U-FRW-037-054): "holds every row while a legal hold is in force" and "removes expired rows once the hold is lifted" run `runEvidencePurge` (`packages/authority/src/workflow/evidence-purge-job.ts`) over table `workflow_evidence` (migration `services/document-service/migrations/0060_workflow_evidence.sql`, adapter `services/document-service/src/workflow/evidence-store.ts`) and read the `audit_events` row, which holds only `removedCount`, `keptCount` and `legalHold`. Criteria 2 and 4 are ticked on that evidence. The hold is still the caller's input: no document-level hold is stored yet.

**Evidence (criteria 3 and 4, lane w4, 2026-11-18):** `services/document-service/test/postgres/frw-043.erasure.pg.test.ts` (3 tests, PostgreSQL in Podman, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, passed; the database is made for the file and dropped afterwards). `eraseParticipant` (`services/document-service/src/workflow/participant-erasure.ts`, adapters in `participant-erasure-pg.ts`) deletes one participant's rows of `workflow_evidence` and replaces the payloads of the commits that person wrote field values in (scope `field:<id>`) with tombstones through `CommitTombstone` (HIS-040). The test shows that the other participant's rows and commits, and the same person's body commit, are untouched; the commit metadata rows are byte-for-byte as before and `logs.verify` returns the same verdict; a document under a legal hold is refused with `conflict` and nothing changes; a commit above the newest checkpoint is refused before anything changes. The audit entry `evidence.erased` holds `removedCount` and `tombstonedCount` and none of the participant identifier, the surrogate, the address or the user agent; the tombstone entries carry the fixed reason and the sequence number. The purge half of criterion 4 was ticked on the evidence above.

**Evidence (criterion 1, lane V-FRW-037-054, 2026-11-13):** `services/document-service/test/postgres/frw-043.v-frw-037-054.purge-sealed.pg.test.ts` (1 test, PostgreSQL and the Object Lock bucket of the Versity S3 Gateway in Podman, run with `pnpm vitest run` on that file, database and bucket named with V-FRW-037-054 and removed afterwards): three commits verify before and after a purge that removes two expired `workflow_evidence` rows; the commit log, the head and `logs.verify` are unchanged; the sealed PDF written by `storeAndRecordSealedPdf` reads back with the recorded hash, the record is unchanged, and an overwrite and a delete are still refused.

**Notes:** Lane w4 (2026-11-26): criterion 5 is ticked in a reworded form. The owner's rule (decision 31) names one retention and no tenant choice, and ADR 0394 item 5 (no tenant-chosen period in version 1) is a proposed default that decisions 196 to 220 accept in bulk (the closing paragraph of the decisions file does not list it as open). So the original wording ("the retention period is a tenant setting ... changed through the administration API") describes a feature that version 1 does not have; the criterion now says what the decision gives, and it is tested. If the owner later wants a tenant period, `planPurge` already takes one, and the setting would be a new story with its bounds; the administration API pattern of FRW-042 (`/workflow/capture-settings`) would carry it. 12 §7, §15 and 08 §2 are changed to say the point is settled. Earlier, lane w4 (2026-11-18): the default retention is decided (decision 31, FRW-084, ADR 0394): the life of the document, so `planPurge` takes `retentionMs: null` and removes nothing by age. Criterion 3 and the erasure half of criterion 4 are built and ticked (see Evidence). Criterion 5 was left open then, for the reason above. Not built, so reported here: `eraseParticipant` is not routed (no operation in the protocol yet, and the caller gives `surrogateOf`, the map from a participant to the person who authored their commits); under a hold it answers `conflict` and does not queue the erasure as decision 38 says for a person (`ErasureRequests` does that for a person; the participant erasure is asked again after the hold is released); checkpoints at or after a tombstoned commit still hold the text (the result lists them, as HIS-040 does). Phase inferred (evidence arrives in phase 2). The default retention period was an open owner question (FRW-084). ADR 0011 consequence: erasing one value means tombstoning commit payloads. Authority side, lane authority-4 (round 2, 2026-11-13): `packages/authority/src/workflow/retention.ts` (`planPurge`) decides which rows a purge may remove, keeps all rows under a legal hold, and writes an audit entry of counts and the instant only; tested in `packages/authority/test/retention.test.ts`. Not met, so unticked: criterion 1 (the chain check and the untouched commit log and sealed PDF are not tested; the PDF is not built) and criterion 3 (the erasure with tombstones is not built); criterion 5 waits for FRW-084, so no default is set and the caller passes `retentionMs`. Open, not decided: the instant the retention clock starts from. Lane U-FRW-037-054 (2026-11-13): the purge job now runs over PostgreSQL (criteria 2 and 4, see Evidence). The purge is not scheduled; the caller runs it. `test/postgres/schema.pg.test.ts` lists the tables and counts of the migrations and fails on them: it does not list 0050's `threads` and `versions`, and now not `workflow_evidence` either. That file is not this lane's, so it is left as it is and reported. Lane V-FRW-019-036: criterion 1 is tested in part on PostgreSQL in Podman by `services/document-service/test/postgres/v-frw-019-036.purge-chain.pg.test.ts` (1 test): a purge removes the two expired evidence rows, and the commit log, its head and `logs.verify` are the same as before. The sealed PDF is not built (FRW-049, FRW-050), so criterion 1 stays unticked; criterion 3 (erasure with tombstones) is not built.
