Lane V-FRW-037-054 (2026-11-13): the sealed PDF of FRW-054 now exists in the store, so criterion 1 is ticked on the evidence above; the sealed PDF step is the store's, not the export's. Criterion 3 is still not built. Evidence audit (2026-11-13): criterion 4 was unticked because its erasure half has no code and no test (only the purge writes an audit entry, with counts and no identifiers), so it is met for a purge only; criterion 2 stays ticked on the job's `legalHold` input, but the stored holds of `control-plane/holds.ts` are not read by the purge and nothing yet calls `runEvidencePurge` in a service.

## Signing ceremony (12 §8.1)

### FRW-044 Accept the consent text before signing

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Participant |

**Spec:** 12 §6.1 (`consent`), §7, §8.1 step 1, §10 · **Requirements:** R13, R11 · **Depends on:** FRW-025, FRW-038, FRW-040

As a participant, I want to read and accept the consent text before I sign, so that I know what I am agreeing to and my acceptance is on record.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given I begin the ceremony, then the consent text named by the workflow's `consent` (`textId` and `version`) is shown before any signing action, and signing is unavailable until I accept it by an explicit action that is not pre-selected.
- [x] Given I accept, when the client calls the participant's consent endpoint, then the service records the text identifier, the version, the hash of the text it holds and the server time, and the client cannot supply the hash.
- [x] Given a sign request from a participant with no recorded acceptance, then it is refused.
- [x] Given a screen reader or keyboard only, then the text is readable in full and the accept control is reachable and named.
- [x] Given I do not accept, then I can decline instead (FRW-033).

**Evidence (criteria 1, 4 and 5, lane w4, 2026-11-26):** Route: `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-044.consent.test.ts` (14 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman project `docedit-test-w4`, passed): `GET .../participants/{pid}/consent` (`readConsent` in `consent-service.ts`) gives the participant the text, identifier and version the service holds, with `acceptedAt` null until they accept and null again if the text changes; other participants, the owner and the service credential are 403, an approver, an access code not yet given, a draft, no consent and a missing text are 409. Port and service: `packages/backend-dep/src/frw-035.workflow-port.universal.test.ts` (the `consent` call is the GET with the bearer token) and `packages/element/test/frw-044-consent.test.ts` (8 tests: the acceptance is a `consent` call with no body, a decline carries its trimmed reason, a blank one calls nothing, no participant calls nothing). Screen: `packages/ui/e2e/frw-044-consent-step.spec.ts` (7 tests, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 21 runs, passed with `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57744 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/frw-044-consent-step.spec.ts`; the neighbouring `frw-045-adoption-step.spec.ts` still passes, 30 runs) and `packages/ui/test/frw-044.consent-step.test.ts` (model, Node.js): the text is shown in full before any signing action, in a region named "Consent text"; no adoption, mode, sign button or kind switch exists until the participant presses "I accept this text"; nothing is checked or pre-selected; the step works from the keyboard (focus starts on the text, Tab reaches accept, decline and cancel, Enter accepts) and the controls are found by role and name; axe finds nothing; "I decline" opens a reason field, a blank reason is refused and nothing is sent, and a reason declines with the reason and offers no signing. A text that cannot be read blocks signing and offers a retry; a refused acceptance says so and signing stays absent. The stand-in service of the browser tests is `packages/ui/e2e/harness/frw-044-consent-port.ts`. Decisions in `docs/adr/0427-w4-FRW-044-consent-step-screen.md` (proposed).

**Evidence (criterion 2, lane w4, 2026-11-18):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-044.consent.test.ts` (10 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, passed). `POST /documents/{id}/workflow/participants/{pid}/consent` (`participantConsent` in `services/document-service/src/control-plane/workflow-routes.ts`, service `acceptConsent` in `src/workflow/consent-service.ts`) records the text identifier, the version, the SHA-256 the service computed over the text it holds and the service time as the `consent` item of the participant's evidence. The test reads the stored row back, compares the hash with an independent SHA-256 of the text, shows that a body with a hash, a time or a text is `validation_failed` and records nothing, that another participant's token, the owner's token and the service credential are `forbidden`, that an approver, an unverified access code, a draft, a workflow with no consent and a missing text are `conflict`, and that a repeat writes no second row. The check a sign request makes (`consentVerdict`) is tested there too: no acceptance, another participant and a changed text are refused.

**Evidence (criterion 3, lane w4, 2026-11-18):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-046.sign.test.ts`, "refuses what P7 and the consent forbid" (memory and PostgreSQL): a sign request from a participant who has not accepted is `forbidden` and writes nothing; after `POST .../consent` the same participant signs. The sign route (`sign-service.ts`) calls `consentVerdict` before it writes.

**Notes (lane w4, fourth pass, 2026-11-26):** All five criteria are ticked. The reasons of the third pass are stale: the signing dialog of FRW-045 and FRW-046 now exists in `packages/ui` and `packages/element`, so the consent step is built into it, and the text reaches the client through a new read route (ADR 0427, proposed). What is still not done: no real screen reader (NVDA, JAWS, VoiceOver) was run, so criterion 4 is met by the accessibility tree (role, name, description) in three engines, axe and the keyboard; where consent texts are authored, stored and localised is still open (ADR 0395 item 1; the store has a memory adapter only); the browser test runs the dialog against a stand-in service, and the layers beneath it are tested apart (route, port adapter, element service), with no single browser run through a real service. Outside the lane: the protocol operation `getParticipantConsent` and its generated files, `WorkflowPort.consent`, the permissions matrix, the TCK pending list, `LATE_KEY_HEADS`. Earlier (third pass, 2026-11-18, blocked): nothing new is ticked. Checked against the owner's decisions (2026-11-15 and 2026-11-17): decision 184 accepts the proposals of this story's Notes (already applied) and nothing else in the file names the consent text, the ceremony screens or the decline control. Criteria 1, 4 and 5 are screens: the text with an accept control that is not pre-selected, the reading by screen reader and keyboard, and the decline control. None exists in `packages/ui` or `packages/element` (the ceremony of FRW-045 criteria 1, 4 and 6, FRW-046 criterion 1 and FRW-048 is unbuilt), so there is nothing to run a keyboard or screen reader check against. Criterion 1 also needs the text to reach the client, and it cannot: the service has no read route for it (the consent route takes no body and returns no text) and the texts have a memory store only, so where texts are authored, stored and localised is still the owner's to design. A route and a table would decide that here. This stays open until the ceremony screens (FRW-045 and FRW-046 UI criteria) and the home of the texts are decided.

**Notes:** Lane w4 (2026-11-18, ADR 0395, proposed): criterion 2 is ticked; the texts come from a port (`ConsentTextStore`) with a memory adapter only, so a real deployment has no way yet to put a text in: a table, a host route to write texts and localisation are not built and are the owner's to design (not decided). Criterion 3 was met in the check (`consentVerdict`, tested) and is ticked now that the sign route of FRW-046 calls it. Criteria 1, 4 and 5 are the editor's (the screen with the text, the accept control that is not pre-selected, screen reader and keyboard use, and the decline control); the editor has no signing ceremony yet, so they stay open. The proposal of the Notes (a changed text refuses an earlier acceptance) is applied as accepted by decision 184. Where consent texts are stored, authored and localised is not specified, and nor is enforcement of consent at the sign endpoint (inferred from "the acceptance is recorded"); see Open questions. Authority side, lane authority-4 (round 2, 2026-11-13): `packages/authority/src/workflow/consent.ts` gives `recordConsent` (the hash is computed on the server from the held text; the function takes no client hash) and `checkConsentForSign` (refuses without an acceptance, for another participant, version or text). Tested in `packages/authority/test/consent.test.ts`. Not met, so nothing is ticked: criteria 1 and 5 are the client's, criterion 2 and 3 need the consent and sign endpoints, which are not built, and criterion 4 is the client's. Proposal, not decided: a changed text refuses an earlier acceptance (ADR 0148, item 1).

### FRW-045 Adopt a signature

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Participant |

**Spec:** 12 §8.1 step 2, §3.3, §11; 05 §6.2 · **Requirements:** R13, R11 · **Depends on:** FRW-044, PRO-061, PRO-062

As a participant, I want to adopt a signature by typing it, drawing it or uploading it, so that I can sign in the way that suits me, and never have to draw.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the adoption step, then "typed" is selected by default and works from the keyboard; "drawn" and "uploaded" are offered as alternatives.
- [x] Given I choose "uploaded", then the image goes through the asset worker like any other upload (quarantine, validation, re-encoding, hashing, storage) and cannot be used until `asset.ready`; given `asset.failed`, then it is refused with a message.
- [x] Given I adopt a signature, then it becomes an asset attached to the document and the value that will be written is `{ asset, mode }` with `mode` `typed`, `drawn` or `uploaded`; the hash of the image is recorded in the evidence (FRW-040).
- [x] Given I also need initials, then the same step adopts them separately.
- [x] Given a participant with `fill`, then they may upload the asset (upload needs `edit`, `suggest` or `fill` somewhere in the document).
- [x] Given the typed signature, then the preview is shown in text form to assistive technology.

**Evidence:** `packages/authority/test/matrix.test.ts`, "reads "somewhere in the document" as at least one scope": a connection whose role grants `fill` on an inherit region is allowed `asset.upload`, and a commenter is refused. The row is `asset.upload` (row 6) in `packages/authority/src/permissions/matrix.ts`.

**Evidence (criterion 2, lane w4, 2026-11-18):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-045.signature-adoption.test.ts` (3 tests, memory storage with the asset worker in process, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, passed). A PNG uploaded with `POST /documents/{id}/assets` and completed goes through the worker; `adoptSignature` (`services/document-service/src/workflow/signature-adoption.ts`) refuses it while it is `quarantined`, answers `{ asset, mode }` and the SHA-256 of the stored bytes once it is `ready` (equal to the worker's recorded hash), and refuses an asset the worker failed with the worker's own reason. It also refuses another person's image, an image of another document and an unknown identifier. The screen that offers the three modes and shows the failure message is the editor's.

**Evidence (criterion 3, lane w4, 2026-11-18):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-046.sign.test.ts`, "writes the signature and the dateSigned fields of the role in one commit": the signing commit writes `{ asset, mode }` and attaches the image to the document (`attachedAssets` names the asset at the signing `seq`), and the evidence row holds the mode and the SHA-256 of the stored bytes, which the test recomputes. The adoption is checked at signing (ADR 0396, ADR 0397).
**Evidence (criteria 1, 4 and 6, lane w4, third pass, 2026-11-18):** The step is `packages/ui/src/components/SignatureDialog.svelte` (opened by `App.svelte` when the core's `signing.request` names a field; the model is `packages/ui/src/shell/signature-adoption.ts`, the service type `SigningService` in `core-api.ts`, the words in `i18n/en-GB.ts`). `packages/ui/e2e/frw-045-adoption-step.spec.ts` (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit on this machine, 2026-11-18, 30 passed; `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57645 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/frw-045-adoption-step.spec.ts`), with a stand-in service (`e2e/harness/frw-045-signing-port.ts`). Criterion 1: "Type it" is the selected mode when the step opens and is the focused control; "Draw it" and "Upload an image" are offered beside it as radio buttons that the arrow keys move among; the whole path (type, adopt, sign) runs from the keyboard; adopting with nothing typed, drawn or chosen is refused in words; the drawn image and the uploaded file reach the service as PNG; drawing is never required, and the hint says to choose "Type it" if the person cannot draw; axe reports no violations. Criterion 4: the same step switches to the initials, which start empty and are adopted on their own (file `initials-typed.png`, not the signature's); an initials field is not offered the signature adopted earlier. Criterion 6: the typed name is real text in the step ("Preview, as text", tidied), the field is described by it (`aria-describedby`), and the picture drawn from it is not the only form. `packages/ui/test/frw-045.signature-adoption.test.ts` (6 tests: the default mode, what each mode needs, the tidied text, the initials). The step reaches the participant through the editor with FRW-046 criterion 1.

**Notes (lane w4, third pass, 2026-11-18):** Criteria 1, 4 and 6 are ticked; all six are ticked. The typed name is drawn to a PNG by the client (canvas, a cursive font stack: `"Segoe Script", "Brush Script MT", cursive, serif`) and uploaded like the other modes, which is the accepted proposal of decision 184 (ADR 0396); which font to ship is a design choice that is not the owner's and is open. The step is run in a browser against a stand-in service; its service over the backend ports is FRW-046.

**Notes:** Lane w4 (2026-11-18, ADR 0396, proposed): the typed name is drawn by the client and uploaded, so the three modes share one server path (the accepted proposal of decision 184); the service renders no text. Criterion 2 is ticked on the service half above. Criterion 3 is ticked with FRW-046: the value is written and the hash enters the evidence at signing. Criteria 1, 4 and 6 are the editor's (the default mode, the keyboard path, the second step for initials, the text preview for assistive technology); the editor has no adoption step. How a typed name becomes the `asset` the value requires (which font, rendered by whom) is not specified (Open questions). Authority-4 (2026-11-13): criterion 3 is partly met (the value shape `{ asset, mode }` is validated in `packages/model/src/fields/values.ts`), but the image hash in the evidence needs FRW-040 and the asset store, so it stays open. Criteria 1, 2, 4 and 6 are the editor's and the asset worker's.

### FRW-046 Sign signature and initials fields

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Participant |

**Spec:** 12 §8.1 step 3, §3.3, §4.2 (P7, P8), §10, §14; ADR 0013 · **Requirements:** R13, R10 · **Depends on:** FRW-009, FRW-022, FRW-044, FRW-045

As a participant, I want to sign each of my signature and initials fields, so that the signature is written by the server and cannot be forged or backdated.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given I have accepted the consent and adopted a signature, when I activate a signature or initials field assigned to my role, then the component calls `POST /documents/{id}/fields/{fid}/sign`.
- [x] Given the call, then the service checks the P7 conditions for that field (the assignee is one of my roles, I am active, the field is visible and enabled) and that its kind is `signature` or `initials`; given any condition fails, then the request is refused.
- [x] Given the checks pass, then the service writes the field's `{ asset, mode }` in a server-authored commit with origin `sign` and, in the same commit, sets every `dateSigned` field assigned to the same role to the current date in the workflow's `timeZone`, taken from the server clock and never from the client.
- [x] Given the signing, then the evidence records the `seq` and chain hash of the signing commit (FRW-040), and `field_completed` is raised.
- [x] Given the same request is repeated with the same `Idempotency-Key`, then one commit is written.
- [x] Given a field of another role, a participant who is not active, or a field hidden by a rule, then the request is refused and nothing is written.
- [x] Given a `dateSigned` date near midnight where the workflow's time zone differs from the server's, then the stored date is the date in the workflow's time zone.

**Evidence:** `packages/authority/test/sign.test.ts`, "a dateSigned value is the date in the workflow time zone (FRW-046 criterion 7)": `signedDate` (`packages/authority/src/permissions/sign.ts`) gives 2026-11-07 for 2026-11-06 23:30 UTC in Asia/Tokyo and 2026-11-06 in America/Los_Angeles; a property over 1970 to 2100 checks the UTC case against the ISO date. The write of the value by the signing commit is not built (see Notes). Unticked by the round 1 review: `signedDate` is imported only by its own test, no commit stores the date, so the criterion about the stored date is not met yet.

**Evidence (criteria 2 to 7, lane w4, 2026-11-18):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-046.sign.test.ts` (6 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman, real data plane and asset worker, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, passed). `POST /documents/{id}/fields/{fid}/sign` (`signField` in `src/control-plane/workflow-routes.ts`, service `src/workflow/sign-service.ts`). Criteria 2 and 6: with no consent, a field of another role, a field hidden by a rule, a field of another kind, a field that does not exist, a participant who is not active, another person's image, a bad mode, an extra member and the host's service credential, the answer is a refusal and the log has no `sign` commit, the head is as it was, the field is empty, no evidence row and no event. Criterion 3: one commit with origin `sign`, attributed to the participant, sets the signature and the `tenant` role's `dateSigned` field (the landlord's stays empty); the image is attached to the document (`attachedAssets`). Criterion 4: one `signed` evidence row holds the `seq` and the chain hash of the commit (equal to the log's chain head at that `seq`), the mode and the SHA-256 of the stored image, and `dep.workflow.field_completed.v1` is raised once with that `seq` and chain hash. Criterion 5: the same request with the same `Idempotency-Key`, and without one, answers the same and leaves one commit, one evidence row and one event. Criterion 7: with the server clock moved to 23:30 UTC and the workflow zone `Asia/Tokyo`, the stored date is the Tokyo date, which differs from the UTC date. Unit level: `packages/authority/test/sign.test.ts` (`checkSignConditions`, property over every combination), `services/document-service/src/data-plane/asset-attachments.test.ts`.
**Evidence (criterion 1, lane w4, third pass, 2026-11-18):** Activating a field is tested in four parts that meet at the same objects; no run joins them against a live server. (1) The field in the document: `packages/engine/src/view/form-field-view.ts` draws a `form_field` of kind `signature` or `initials` as a button (`SignFieldView`): "Sign signature" or "Sign initials" when the participant may sign it, "Signed" once it has a value, "Not signed" otherwise, in words and not by colour; a click, Enter or Space asks the core for the step. `packages/engine/src/view/frw-046-sign-field.universal.test.ts` (4 tests in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 12 passed, `pnpm exec vitest run --project browser <file>`, this machine, 2026-11-18). (2) The core: `fieldSignable` and `requestSign` (`packages/engine/src/core.ts`) allow a field of a role the active participant holds, refuse another role, a read-only field, another kind, a participant who is not active and a field a rule hides, and make a request that names the field, its kind, its label and its role by the document's own name: `packages/engine/test/frw-046.sign-request.test.ts` (7 tests). (3) The step: `SignatureDialog` opens on the request and signs with the adoption (FRW-045; `packages/ui/e2e/frw-045-adoption-step.spec.ts`, 30 passed in three browsers). (4) The call: `signingService` (`packages/element/src/signing.ts`, wired by `editor.ts` and `doc-editor.ts` when the backend has the assets and fields ports) uploads the image through the assets port and calls `fields.sign(docId, fieldId, { asset, mode })`, and says a refusal in a word (consent, not active, forbidden): `packages/element/test/frw-046-signing.test.ts` (6 tests, stand-in ports). The `fields` port of `backend-dep` (`packages/backend-dep/src/fields.ts`, present while `fields` is negotiated) posts that body to `POST /documents/{id}/fields/{fid}/sign` with the bearer token: `packages/backend-dep/src/frw-046.fields-port.universal.test.ts` (2 tests). The route itself is the service's and is tested under criteria 2 to 7 above.

**Notes (lane w4, third pass, 2026-11-18):** All seven criteria are ticked. The consent is the server's check at signing (refused as `consent`, said in words); the screen that shows the text and takes the acceptance is FRW-044 criterion 1, which is not built, so a participant cannot yet accept the consent in the editor and a workflow that has a consent text refuses every sign request until the host's page has recorded the acceptance. That is a gap in the ceremony, not in this criterion. A signature field now draws as a button for everyone, including the owner who prepares the document (it shows "Not signed" and cannot be activated); the image of a signed field is not drawn yet. Which font draws a typed name is open (FRW-045 Notes). Outside the lane: `packages/engine/src/core.ts`, `api.ts`, `view/web.ts` (additive), `packages/engine/test/api.test.ts` (four members added to the allowed list), `packages/backend-dep/src/backend.ts` (the `fields` port).

**Notes:** Lane w4 (2026-11-18, ADR 0397, proposed): criteria 2 to 7 are ticked on the evidence above. Criterion 1 stays open: the component that calls the route is the editor's and does not exist. Decisions taken and reported in ADR 0397: the request and answer shape (`{ asset, mode }`), no `head` precondition on the commit (the room keeps a queued control commit ahead of the stored head, observed on memory and PostgreSQL, which is the room's behaviour and not changed here), `Idempotency-Key` added to the operation (`spec/dep` regenerated), a later signing sets the role's `dateSigned` fields again (the open question below), and the evidence hash is not yet in the chain. Changes outside this lane: `assetRefsIn` (data plane) names the image of a signature value, `MemoryLogStore.chainHeadAt`, and exports of the authority index. Authority side, lane authority-4: `checkSign` (P7 conditions with the signature and initials kinds), `fieldsToDateSign` (the dateSigned fields of the role) and `signedDate` are in `packages/authority/src/permissions/sign.ts`, tested in `packages/authority/test/sign.test.ts`. Not met: criteria 1 to 6, because the sign endpoint, the server-authored commit with origin `sign`, the refusal response, the `seq` and chain hash in the evidence and the idempotency of a repeated request are not built; criterion 2 and criterion 4 are checked by `checkSign` only. Pitfall (12 §14): time. P7's kind exclusion applies to fills, so the sign endpoint checks the other P7 conditions. When a role has several signature fields, each sign commit sets the `dateSigned` fields of that role; confirm whether a later signing overwrites the date.

### FRW-047 Complete my part of the workflow

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Participant |

**Spec:** 12 §5.3, §6.2, §8.1 step 4, §10 · **Requirements:** R13 · **Depends on:** FRW-022, FRW-030, FRW-031, FRW-035, FRW-036, FRW-046

As a participant, I want to complete my part, so that the workflow moves on and the document keeps only valid values.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given I am active and call the participant's `/complete` endpoint, then the service checks the required fields (static `required` and the `require` effects) and the validations (static limits, patterns and `validate` effects) of the fields visible to my roles; given any fails, then completion is refused with a problem listing each field key and the rule or validation, and I stay active.
- [x] Given every check passes, then in one server-authored commit with origin `rules` the service writes the formula results and clears the values of hidden fields, and then marks me completed with `completedSeq` and a control commit.
- [x] Given a hidden field that holds a value, then after completion its value is null.
- [x] Given I complete, then `participant_completed` is raised, the next order is activated if any, and the last participant's completion moves the workflow to `completed`.
- [x] Given the `rules` commit is retried or the process stops in between, then it is applied exactly once.
- [x] Given I have already completed, then a second call changes nothing.
- [x] Given a participant of kind `approver`, then completing is approving; there are no fields to check.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-047.complete.test.ts` (3 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman, real data plane, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, passed). `POST /documents/{id}/workflow/participants/{pid}/complete` (`completeRoute` in `src/control-plane/workflow-routes.ts`, service `src/workflow/complete-service.ts`). Criterion 1: with an empty required field and a value that fails its pattern the answer is 400 `validation_failed` with one violation for `/fields/tenant.name` (`required`) and one for `/fields/tenant.code` (`pattern`), none for the other role's fields; the log head is as it was, no commit, no evidence row, no event, and the participant is still `active`. Criteria 2 and 3: one commit with origin `rules`, attributed to the participant, sets the formula field to 6 and clears the hidden field that held a value; a field of the other role is unchanged; the participant is `completed` with `completedSeq` equal to that commit's `seq`; one evidence row `completed`. Criterion 4: `participant_completed` once, `participant_activated` for the next order, and the last completion (the approver) moves the workflow to `completed` and raises `completed` once, with a control commit after it. Criterion 5: the workflow store is made to fail once after the `rules` commit; the request answers 500, the commit is stored and the participant is still active; the repeat completes the participant and the log holds one `rules` commit. Criterion 6: a second call answers the same participants, writes no commit, no event and no evidence row, also after the workflow ended. Criterion 7: the approver completes with no fields to check. Also refused: a participant whose turn it is not, another participant's token, the host's service credential and a body with members. Unit level: `packages/authority/test/frw-047.clear-hidden.test.ts` (`completionWrites`; the outcome check lets only a server-authored `rules` commit clear a hidden value), `packages/authority/test/completion.test.ts`.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, ADR 0398, proposed):** All seven criteria are ticked. Reading to confirm: criterion 2's "control commit" is the one the transition writes when it changes the lock (FRW-030), so a completion that leaves the workflow `in_progress` has the `rules` commit and no control commit, and the last one has both; the test shows the control commit after the last completion. Change outside this lane: `outcomeCheck` (`packages/authority/src/permissions/outcome.ts`) lets a server-authored `rules` commit set a field value to null, because P7 would refuse clearing a hidden field. Decisions taken and reported in ADR 0398: the refusal is `validation_failed` with a violation per field key; a `sent` workflow is `conflict`; `completedSeq` is the `rules` commit's `seq`, or the head when nothing was written; the hidden fields cleared are those hidden under the completing participant's roles; a stop after the transition and before the evidence row loses that row. Earlier notes follow.

**Earlier notes:** Lane FRW-040-052, round 1 (2026-11-13): `packages/authority/test/completion.approver.test.ts` checks criterion 7's check: an approver role that owns no field passes while a tenant's required field is empty, and the tenant still refuses. No criterion ticked, because the `/complete` endpoint and the approve action are not built. Authority side, lane authority-4: `completionCheck` (required, static validation and `validate` messages of the participant's visible fields, through `@docedit/rules`) and `hiddenValuesToClear` are in `packages/authority/src/workflow/completion.ts`, tested in `packages/authority/test/completion.test.ts`; no criterion is ticked because the `/complete` endpoint, the `rules` commit that writes the formula results and clears hidden values, the refusal response, the `participant_completed` event and the exactly-once retry are not built. 12 §5.3 says the check covers "required fields and validations" and does not say whether that means the participant's fields or all fields; the criteria read it as the participant's own fields, because later participants' fields are still empty. A `completed` version is created at the final `completedSeq` (08 §4). The code of the refusal problem is not named by 12 (the registry has `validation_failed`).

### FRW-048 Sign without drawing or a pointer

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Participant |

**Spec:** 12 §11 (accessibility), §14; PRODUCT (accessibility) · **Requirements:** R13, R11 · **Depends on:** FRW-044, FRW-045, FRW-046, FRW-047

As a participant who uses only a keyboard or a screen reader, I want to complete the whole ceremony without drawing or using a pointer, so that signing does not exclude me.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given only a keyboard, when I read and accept the consent, choose a typed signature, sign every signature and initials field and complete, then every step is reachable and operable, focus is visible and never trapped, and Escape leaves the editing surface.
- [x] Given a screen reader, then each step announces its result through a live region (consent accepted, "signed 2 of 3", completed), and every control has an accessible name.
- [x] Given the default signature mode is typed, then no step requires drawing and the drawing canvas has a typed alternative.
- [x] Given a signed, unsigned, required or other-role field, then its state is conveyed by text or icon as well as colour, and in `forced-colors` mode system colours are used.
- [x] Given one-time or access-code inputs that the component hosts, then they accept paste.
- [x] Given every ceremony screen, then axe reports no violations, controls meet the target-size criterion, and the layout reflows at 200 per cent zoom in the web view.

**Evidence (criteria 3 and 5, lane w4, 2026-11-18):** `packages/ui/e2e/frw-045-adoption-step.spec.ts` and `packages/ui/e2e/frw-039-access-code.spec.ts` in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit on this machine (43 passed, 2 skipped by the specs themselves: axe outside Chromium on the access-code page; `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57648 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/frw-039-access-code.spec.ts packages/ui/e2e/frw-045-adoption-step.spec.ts`). Criterion 3: the adoption step opens with "Type it" selected and focused, the whole path (type, adopt, sign) runs from the keyboard, adopting with nothing typed, drawn or chosen is refused in words, and the step's hint says to choose "Type it" when the person cannot draw; nothing else in the ceremony that exists asks for a drawing. Criterion 5: the access-code field the component hosts (`AccessCodeForm.svelte`, FRW-039) has no paste handler, a paste event is not cancelled, and text inserted as a paste does is submitted (trimmed); the host's own one-time passcode pages are the host's (12 §7).

**Evidence (criteria 1, 2 and 6, lane w4, 2026-11-26):** packages/ui/e2e/frw-048-ceremony-a11y.spec.ts (8 tests, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 24 runs, passed with `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57748 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/frw-048-ceremony-a11y.spec.ts`; the consent and adoption specs still pass beside it, 73 runs in all) and packages/element/test/frw-048-ceremony.test.ts (6 tests, Node.js). Criterion 1: one test runs the whole path with the keyboard only: the consent text has focus, Tab reaches accept and Enter accepts; typed is selected and focused; type, adopt and sign with Tab and Enter for the signature and then for the initials field (the consent is not asked again); the Finish control is reached by Tab and Enter completes. Focus shows an outline or shadow on accept, adopt, sign and finish; Escape closes the step at the consent stage and at the adoption stage, Tab stays inside the open dialog, and the signing service is told of each dismissal. Criterion 2: a `role="status"` live region says "consent accepted", "signed 1 of 3", "signed 2 of 3" and "completed" in turn, says how many fields still need attention or that the part cannot be completed now, and says what the consent step is doing while it works; the controls of the consent text, the buttons and the reason field are named (axe finds no unnamed control). Criterion 6: on every screen of the ceremony (consent, adoption, signing, completion) axe reports nothing, every enabled control is at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels (WCAG 2.5.8), and the dialog stays inside the viewport with no sideways scroll at 640 CSS pixels (200 per cent zoom) and at 320 pixels.

**Evidence (criterion 4, lane w4, 2026-11-26):** `packages/engine/src/view/frw-048-sign-states.universal.test.ts` (`pnpm exec vitest run --project browser <file>`, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit from Playwright on this Windows machine, 2 tests in each, passed): the text of a signature or initials button says "Signed", "Sign signature", "Sign signature (required)", "Not signed (another signer)" or "Not signed" (a field no one may sign), the `aria-label` repeats it, `data-state` and `data-required` follow, and the border is dashed when it waits for the signer, solid when signed (with a tick before the word) and dotted when it is not this participant's, so no state is told by colour alone (`SignFieldView` in `packages/engine/src/view/form-field-view.ts`; `.de-sign-button` in `packages/element/src/styles/element.css`). Under forced colours: the same file, 1 test in each engine, run with `pnpm exec vitest run --config tests/w4-FRW-048/vitest.forced-colors.config.ts` (a Playwright context with `forcedColors: 'active'`; 9 tests passed in the three engines): the button uses the system colours (`ButtonText` on `ButtonFace`, `GrayText` when disabled), keeps its border and line style, and keeps the words. The role markers on the fill pane's rows are FRW-074's (contrast and forced colours, `packages/ui/e2e/frw-074-markers.spec.ts`); a rule's `require` does not reach the signature button's wording, only the field's own `required` flag does.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-26):** All six criteria are ticked. The two notes below are older: the screens they say are missing now exist (FRW-044 to FRW-046) and criteria 1, 2 and 6 were met on them; criterion 4 is met by the signature button's words and line styles (see its evidence). The earlier Evidence line was cut off in the middle of a sentence and is completed above.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, third pass, partial):** Criteria 3 and 5 are ticked on the screens that exist. Criteria 1, 2, 4 and 6 stay unticked. The adoption and signing step (`SignatureDialog`) and the signature field's button now exist (FRW-045, FRW-046), but the two screens the criteria name around them do not: the consent text with its accept control (FRW-044 criteria 1, 4 and 5, which waits on where the consent texts are stored and delivered to the client: decision 184 accepts the proposals of the Notes and does not decide that) and the completion step (`completeFilling()` has no screen; the guided bar's Finish control opens the summary). Criterion 1 needs a keyboard run through consent and completion; criterion 2 needs live regions for "consent accepted", "signed 2 of 3" and "completed" (the dialog's own region says only the adoption and signing results); criterion 6 says "every ceremony screen". Criterion 4 (state by text as well as colour, `forced-colors`): the signature button says "Sign signature", "Signed" or "Not signed" in words and draws its border in `currentColor`, but "other-role" and "required" are not told apart in text on that button, and no browser run under `forced-colors: active` of the sign field was made, so it is not ticked. The screens would be built under FRW-044, not here.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, blocked):** No criterion is ticked. Every criterion describes the screens of the signing ceremony: the consent text and its accept control, the adoption step with typed as the default, the sign and initials steps and the completion. None of those screens exists. The editor has no ceremony (FRW-044 criteria 1 and 4, FRW-045 criteria 1, 4 and 6 and FRW-046 criterion 1 are open for that reason), and the host's participant pages (UX-099 criterion 7) are not built. The service half is built and tested: consent, adoption, signing and completion (`POST .../consent`, `POST /documents/{id}/fields/{fid}/sign`, `POST .../complete`; FRW-044 to FRW-047). There is nothing to run a keyboard path, a live region, axe or a zoom check against, and building the screens here would be building those three stories' unticked criteria under this identifier, with decisions that are open (how a typed name becomes the image asset, FRW-045 Notes; where the ceremony is mounted, in the component or in the host's pages). When the screens exist, this story is the accessibility pass over them; the live-region wording, the typed alternative to the drawing canvas and the forced-colors states listed above are its test list.

## Final PDF and sealing (12 §8.2)

### FRW-049 Produce the final PDF at completion

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Owner |

**Spec:** 12 §8.2 step 1, §6.1 (`finalisation`), §6.2, §12 · **Requirements:** R13, R5 · **Depends on:** FRW-047, EXP-033, EXP-004, EXP-070

As an owner, I want the final PDF produced automatically when the last participant completes, so that the result is the document exactly as it stood at completion.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the workflow reaches `completed`, when finalisation triggers, then an export job is created at `completedSeq`, pinned to that `seq`, with field values flattened into the text and, when `finalisation.certificate` is true, certificate pages appended.
- [x] Given the job, then it is the document at `completedSeq` that is typeset, not a later head, and a `completed` version points at it.
- [x] Given the compile fails, then the workflow stays `completed` and not `finalised`, the failure and its diagnostics are visible to the owner and the operator, and `finalised` is not raised.
- [x] Given `completed` is reached, then `completed` is raised; given the sealed PDF is stored, then `finalised` is raised (FRW-054).
- [x] Given `finalisation.certificate` is false, then no certificate pages are appended.

**Evidence:** `packages/authority/test/workflow.finalisation.test.ts`, "raises completed and then finalised when every step succeeds" (criterion 4 at the gate: `completed` is always raised first, `finalised` only once the sealed bytes are stored), and the property "finalised iff compile, seal (timestamped) and store all succeed". The gate is `packages/authority/src/workflow/finalisation.ts` (`finaliseWorkflow`), exported from `packages/authority/src/index.ts` (round 3 fix); the test imports it from the package entry.

**Evidence (criteria 2 and 3, lane w4, 2026-11-18):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-049.final-export.test.ts` (3 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman, real data plane, a handler in place of the compile worker as in `export-jobs.test.ts`, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, passed). `startFinalExport` (`src/workflow/final-export-start.ts`) runs from the complete route when the workflow reaches `completed`. Criterion 2: the job's payload has `seq` equal to the participant's `completedSeq`, `profile: final`, `full: true`, `options { fields: flatten }`; the head is later than that `seq` (the control commit of the lock) and the handler receives the pinned `seq`; the `completed` version is stored at `completedSeq`; a repeated start makes no second job. Criterion 3: with a handler that fails, the job fails with `compile_failed`, `workflow.finalisation_failed` is audited with the job and the code (operator), `dep.export.failed.v1` is raised (host), `export.failed` reaches the owner's open channel and the owner reads the failed job with `GET /exports/{id}`; the workflow resource is still `completed` and `finalised` is not raised. Unit level: `packages/authority/test/final-export.test.ts`.

**Evidence (criteria 1 and 5, lane w4, 2026-11-18):** with FRW-050: the job carries `options.certificate` and, only when it is true, the `certificate` facts (`services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-049.final-export.test.ts`, first and second tests); the worker appends the pages only for a job that carries facts (`services/export-worker/src/frw-050-certificate.job.test.ts`: facts give the pages after the body, `certificate: false` gives none, `certificate: true` without facts is refused).

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criterion 4):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-053.live-seal.test.ts` (6 tests, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman, none skipped): the last completion raises `dep.workflow.completed.v1`; the final export job, the seal and the store then run with no call from the test, and `dep.workflow.finalised.v1` is raised for the document once the sealed PDF is stored; with the authority down it is not raised and the workflow stays `completed`. The events are raised by `raiseWorkflowEvents` from `FinalSealer`.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, ADR 0399, proposed):** Criteria 1, 2, 3 and 5 are ticked (1 and 5 with FRW-050, which makes the worker append the pages; no PDF was compiled here, see its Notes). Criterion 4 stays open: `completed` is raised (FRW-047), but `finalised` waits for the seal and the store (FRW-053, FRW-054). Decisions in ADR 0399: the job belongs to the sender; the budget and the concurrency quota are not applied; no retry and no restart sweep. Earlier notes follow.

**Earlier notes:** Lane FRW-040-052, round 1 (2026-11-13): `packages/authority/src/workflow/final-export.ts` (`finalExportRequest`) gives the export request: pinned to `completedSeq`, fields flattened, profile `final`, certificate pages only when `finalisation.certificate` is true. An absent option reads as false (ADR 0250 item 2; the spec gives no default). Only a `completed` workflow yields a request. Tested in `packages/authority/test/final-export.test.ts`. No criterion ticked: the export job is not created (criterion 1), the typesetting of `completedSeq` and the `completed` version are the export's and the workflow service's (criterion 2), and the certificate pages are EXP's (criterion 5). Evidence audit (2026-11-13): criterion 4 unticked, because no code raises `dep.workflow.completed` or `dep.workflow.finalised` (`finaliseWorkflow` only returns a `raised` list, and only tests call it), and FRW-054 criterion 2, which holds the same "`finalised` is raised" clause, is rightly unticked for that reason. Round 3 review: nothing yet acts on `raised` (`['completed', 'finalised']`); the workflow service that raises the events from it is not built. Criterion 4 is met at the gate only (`finaliseWorkflow` in `packages/authority/src/workflow/finalisation.ts`); the compile, the seal and the store are the export's, the signing service's and the store's. Not met: criteria 1, 2 and 5 (the export job, the pinned `seq`, the certificate pages) and the visibility part of criterion 3 (the diagnostics are returned; showing them is the service's). Failure handling and retry are not specified; the gate stops at the first failed step and returns no retry (Open questions 26; ADR 0148). Compile cost with tagging on is unmeasured (spike S4). Second review (2026-11-06, SPK-027), SR-M3: under ADR 0019 one final PDF with every value flattened would show participants what is hidden from them; the review recommends one sealed full PDF for the owner, a sealed rendering of each participant's projection for that participant, and evidence that records the projection each signer saw (owner decision) ([review](../review/2026-11-06-second-review.md)).

### FRW-050 Add a certificate of completion

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Relying party |

**Spec:** 12 §8.2 step 1, §1.1, §7; 09 §4, §9.1 · **Requirements:** R13, R5, R11 · **Depends on:** FRW-040, FRW-049, FRW-058, EXP-002, EXP-005, EXP-033, EXP-042, EXP-043

As a relying party, I want certificate pages that list the identifiers, the sequence numbers, the chain-head hash and each participant's events, so that I can match the PDF to the evidence.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a completed workflow, then the certificate shows the document and workflow identifiers, `sentSeq`, `completedSeq`, the chain-head hash and, for each participant, their events (viewed, consent, signed, completed, declined) with server times.
- [x] Given the option `certificate`, when the final export of a completed workflow runs, then certificate pages are appended after the body, the body's pagination does not change, and the export uses `completedSeq`, `fields` set to `flatten` and the `final` profile.
- [x] Given the certificate is generated LaTeX built from the evidence, then it is produced only through the typed emitters of 09, with no raw interpolation.
- [x] Given a participant name or any evidence string that contains TeX control sequences or markup, then the certificate compiles and prints it literally.
- [x] Given a tenant has switched off IP address and user agent capture, when the certificate is generated, then those items are absent.
- [x] Given the certificate pages, then they are tagged and accessible like the body.
- [x] Given the certificate, then its wording follows the rules of FRW-058 and describes the authentication of a participant as asserted by the host, never as verified identity.

**Evidence (criteria 1, 2, 3, 5 and 7, lane w4, 2026-11-18, no PDF compiled):** `packages/export-latex/src/frw-050-certificate.test.ts` (6 tests): the converter's TeX holds the identifiers, `sentSeq`, `completedSeq`, the chain head and each event with its server time (criterion 1); every body block's TeX is identical with and without the certificate, the certificate starts with a page break after the last body line, and the option joins the manifest (criterion 2); a name and an evidence string full of control sequences, markup and a `\begin{document}` appear only escaped, so the file has one real one (criterion 3, at the source); with the setting off no address or agent is in the TeX (criterion 5). `services/document-service/src/workflow/certificate-facts.test.ts` (5 tests, `certificateFactsOf`): the facts are built from the evidence rows (viewed, consent, each signing, completed), the address and agent are dropped when the setting is off now, even for rows stored while it was on, and the fixed sentences are within the legal stance and say "as asserted by the host" (criteria 1, 5 and 7). `services/export-worker/src/frw-050-certificate.job.test.ts` (4 tests, fake compile): a job with facts compiles a main.tex with the certificate after the body, `certificate: true` without facts is refused, malformed facts fail the job. `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-049.final-export.test.ts`: the final export job carries the facts built from the real evidence at `completedSeq`, with `fields: flatten` and the `final` profile (criterion 2; memory and PostgreSQL). Each file run with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node`, passed.

**Evidence (criteria 4 and 6, lane w4, 2026-11-26, real compiles):** `packages/export-latex/src/frw-050-certificate.compile.test.ts` (2 tests, Node.js 22.18, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, passed in 240 s on this Windows machine; the compiles run in the export worker's Podman sandbox of EXP-070 and the validation in the veraPDF image of EXP-016; the tests skip when `sandboxReady()` says no, and it said yes here). Criterion 4: a participant label and a user-agent string full of `\textbackslash`, `\end{document}`, `$x^2$`, `%`, `&`, `#`, `_`, `~`, braces, `\input{/etc/passwd}`, `\write18`, `^^41` and `<b>` markup compile in the `final` profile with `certificate` and `fields: flatten`, and `pdftotext` reads both strings back as typed from the certificate pages, after the body text and with no content of `/etc/passwd`. Criterion 6: with `tagging: on` and `pdfStandard: ua-2+a-4f` the PDF with the certificate pages has a structure tree, declares PDF/UA-2, passes veraPDF as PDF/UA-2 and as PDF/A-4f with no failed rule, and carries more marked-content identifiers than the two body paragraphs alone (the certificate's text is tagged).

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-26, ADR 0400, proposed):** All seven criteria are ticked. The compile tests above supersede the open points of the note of 2026-11-18, which said the sandbox was not usable here: it is. The certificate uses bold paragraphs, not heading styles (ADR 0400 point 1); veraPDF accepts that for UA-2, so it stands, and whether a screen reader reads it well has not been tried by a person. The wording of the evidence lines has not been seen by counsel (FRW-087; a human review, not a criterion of this story).

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, ADR 0400, proposed; criteria 4 and 6 were open then):** Criteria 1, 2, 3, 5 and 7 were ticked on the evidence above, which reads the generated TeX and the facts; no certificate had been compiled to a PDF (the local compile tests skipped for want of the font directory, and the sandbox was not usable on this machine). Criterion 4 stayed open: that the pages compile and print the hostile strings literally needs a compile. Criterion 6 stayed open: tagging and accessibility of the pages need a compile and veraPDF; the certificate uses bold paragraphs, not heading styles (ADR 0400 point 1). The live tenant switch for the capture setting (criterion 5) is tested through the in-memory ledger; the route that changes it is FRW-042's. The wording of the evidence lines has not been seen by counsel (FRW-087). Earlier notes follow.

**Earlier notes:** Evidence is personal data (12 §7); what the certificate shows is this epic's decision, and the typesetting rules of the pages are EXP's. This story absorbed EXP-080 during the cross-check. Authority-4 (2026-11-13): `buildCertificateFacts` (`packages/authority/src/workflow/certificate.ts`) gives the facts the pages print, checked and ordered, with the network identity absent while the capture setting is off, and `CERTIFICATE_STATEMENT` gives the fixed sentences; tested by `packages/authority/test/workflow.certificate.test.ts`. Not met: (1) and (6) need the pages, which are EXP's and are not built, so they stay open; the data half is met only. (2) to (4) are the export's (option `certificate`, the `final` profile, typed emitters, escaping of TeX). (5) is the pages' tagging. (7) is met for the fixed sentences only; the wording of the pages is not built and counsel's review (FRW-087) has not taken place.

> FRW-051 (Compare two independent compiles before sealing) was merged into EXP-079 during the cross-check of 2026-11-06.

### FRW-052 Run a signing service that holds the keys

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | L | must | Operator |

**Spec:** 12 §8.2 step 3, §8.3; ADR 0013; 10 §1, §3.6 · **Requirements:** R13, R10 · **Depends on:** SPK-021

As an operator, I want a signing service that is separate from the document service and every worker and that uses keys held in a hardware security module or a cloud key service, so that a stolen worker or a stolen database cannot seal a document.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the deployment, then the signing service runs as its own service in its own trust boundary, and no export or LaTeX worker has a credential for it or access to its keys.
- [ ] Given the signing keys, then they are held in a hardware security module or a cloud key service, are not exportable, and no private key material is present in the service's images, disk or logs.
- [x] Given a request to seal, then only the document service can make it, the request is authenticated, bound to a document, `seq` and PDF hash, rate limited and written to an audit log.
- [x] Given a tenant, then the key used for sealing is selected per tenant from configuration (the operator's key or the tenant's own key).
- [x] Given the signing service is unavailable, then finalisation waits and retries, the workflow stays `completed`, and the operator is alerted.

**Evidence (lane unblock, 2026-11-18, criteria 3 and 4):** the service is `services/signing-service/src/service.ts` (`SigningService`), with `audit.ts`, `rate-limit.ts`, `http.ts` and `main.ts`, on the spike's sealing code (ADR 0411) and the authority's `selectSealingKey` and `checkSealRequest` (now exported from `packages/authority/src/index.ts`). `services/signing-service/test/frw-052-service.test.ts` (21 tests, Node.js 22.18, OpenSSL, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, none skipped; the key is in the test process, so it shows the rules and not the key's place): no or an unknown credential is `unauthenticated` and no key is used; the export worker's own valid credential is `wrong_caller`; bytes that do not hash to the named hash, and a PDF whose two compile hashes are not both its hash, are refused; malformed requests are refused; one document and `seq` is bound to one hash (the same bytes again seal, other bytes are `binding_conflict`); a caller over its limit in a window is `rate_limited` and counts again in the next window; the audit entry `seal.requested` is written before the first key call and `seal.sealed` after it; a failing audit sink gives no seal and no key call; the entries hold only identifiers, hashes and codes. Criterion 4: with a tenant entry in the configuration the tenant's key service signs and the seal's certificate is the one issued for that key, and without one the operator's key signs; a key not in the directory is `no_key`. `services/signing-service/test/frw-052-process.test.ts` (5 tests): `node src/main.ts` runs as a process of its own over HTTP, answers 401 for no or a wrong credential, 403 for the worker's, 400 for a body that is not a request, 413 for a large one, seals for the document service, and writes no secret, key or document byte to its output. `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-053.seal-and-finalise.test.ts` (18 tests: 9 on memory storage and 9 on PostgreSQL in Podman, none skipped) uses the document service's own client (`services/document-service/src/workflow/signing-client.ts`) over HTTP against the service.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criteria 1 and 5):** Criterion 5: `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-053.live-seal.test.ts` (6 tests: 3 on memory storage and 3 on PostgreSQL in Podman, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, none skipped; the real signing service over HTTP on loopback, the key in the test process, a local OpenSSL time-stamp authority). Nothing calls `sealAndFinalise` by hand: the last participant completes, `startFinalExport` queues the job, the export hook starts `FinalSealer.sealCompletedWorkflow` (`src/workflow/final-seal.ts`), and the workflow reads `finalised`. With the authority down the run waits (the delay is slept) and retries, stops with the workflow `completed`, nothing stored, `GET /workflow/final` 404, no `dep.workflow.finalised.v1`; the operator's channel is an `error` log line `workflow.finalisation_stopped` with the document and the code, the counter `docedit_finalisation_stopped_total{reason,code,retryable}` on `/metrics` and the audit entry `workflow.finalisation_failed` (stage `seal`); the scheduled step's function then seals it once the authority answers. A refusal (the worker's credential) is alerted once and not asked again. Criterion 1: `services/signing-service/Containerfile` (Node.js 22.18.0 pinned by digest, user `node`, no default configuration), `Containerfile.dockerignore`, `scripts/build-image.ts` (Podman, `DOCEDIT_CONTAINER_CLI`), and `services/signing-service/test/frw-052-container.podman.test.ts` (4 tests, `DOCEDIT_SIGNING_CONTAINER=1`, Podman 6.0.2 on the WSL machine, none skipped): the image builds, runs as `node`, holds `/app/services/signing-service` and the packages `authority corpus model rules` only (no worker, no other service, no test directory), has no `.pem`, `.key`, `.p12`, `.pfx`, `.cer`, `.crt` or `.jks` file under what it adds, stops with exit code 2 and a message when no configuration is given, and, started with a configuration that has no key service, answers 401 to a request with no credential and 403 to the export worker's own valid one, with the refusals in its audit output and no secret in its logs. What the test does not show: "its own trust boundary" as a network with no route from the compile workers is the deployment's; the worker holds no credential and no key service name (`spk-021-no-key-on-worker.test.ts`), and no compose file or network is added here. ADR 0420 (proposed) records the decisions.

**Notes:** Who the sealing party is (operator or tenant) is an owner decision (SPK, 11 §10 item 11). The key-management details for tenant-owned sealing keys are open (10 §3). Authority-4 (2026-11-13): the pure rules are in `packages/authority/src/workflow/sealing.ts` (`selectSealingKey`, the operator's key unless the tenant has its own, with an empty key refused; `checkSealRequest`, the caller, document, `seq` and PDF hash binding), tested by `packages/authority/test/workflow.sealing.test.ts`. Not met: criterion 4 is the selection only, with no signing service to seal with it; criterion 3 needs the request's authentication, rate limit and audit, which are the service's; criteria 1, 2 and 5 need the service, the key store and the trust boundary, none of which is built here.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, blocked):** No criterion is newly ticked. The owner's decision 14 of 2026-11-15 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md) settles the sealing party: the operator seals by default, and a tenant's own key and certificate are used where the tenant supplies them. That is what `selectSealingKey` already does, so the open question in the Notes above is closed; the criterion that depends on it (4) stays open because no service uses the selection. What is left needs things that do not exist here: (1) and (2) are a deployment and hardware: a signing service in its own trust boundary, and keys in a hardware security module or a cloud key service that cannot be exported; no HSM or key service is reachable from this repository, and a software key store would not meet criterion 2. (3) and (4) need the service itself, which is a new workspace package (\`services/signing-service\`) with its own Containerfile, its credentials, its rate limit and its audit log; adding a workspace package changes \`pnpm-lock.yaml\` and the boundary rules in \`.dependency-cruiser.cjs\`, which this lane may not edit, so the integrator or the owner should create the package first. (5) is the retry of the seal step by finalisation, which does not exist (FRW-053; the gate \`finaliseWorkflow\` is pure). The design the service should follow is in this story's Notes and \`packages/authority/src/workflow/sealing.ts\`: the document service is the only caller (a service credential, checked by \`checkSealRequest\`), the request is bound to the document, \`seq\` and PDF hash, and every request is rate limited and audited. Seal level: PAdES B-LT in phase 2 and B-LTA in phase 4 (decision 15).

**Notes (lane unblock, 2026-11-18, partial, ADR 0412 proposed):** The spike (SPK-021, ADR 0411) and owner decisions 221 to 223 unblocked the build; criteria 3 and 4 are ticked. What stays open, and why. (1) The service is a process of its own with its own credential, and the worker's credential is refused, but "its own trust boundary" is a deployment fact (a network segment with no route from the compile workers) that no test here can show, and no Containerfile is written: no Node.js base image is pinned and the workspace packages need a staged build context (as the export worker's image has). (2) The keys must be in a hardware module or a cloud key service and not exportable; the owner has not chosen one (decision 222), and the SoftHSM2 emulator is software. What the emulator does show, in `frw-052-key-service.podman.test.ts` (6 tests, Podman, `lualatex`, none skipped): the service reaches the key only through `KeyService` and sent 32-byte digests only, the token reports the key as sensitive and never extractable, and no key block is in the audit log or the sealed file. The compile side holds no key service name or credential (`spk-021-no-key-on-worker.test.ts`). (5) Finalisation waits and retries (`sealAndFinalise`, with attempts and delays given by the caller; no default, the specification states no policy) and the workflow stays `completed`, but the alert goes to a port (`OperatorAlert`) and no channel delivers it; the specification names none. The binding of a document and `seq` to one hash is in memory (ADR 0412 point 4). Decisions are in ADR 0412 (wire, authentication by hashed secret, level names, whose clock `/M` is, the retry as parameters).

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-26, blocked):** Nothing new is ticked. The service exists now (`services/signing-service`, its Containerfile and the process test), so the earlier "new workspace package" reason no longer holds. Criterion 2 is the only one open: the keys must sit in a hardware security module or a cloud key service and not be exportable. Owner decisions 221 to 223 allow only a development emulator (SoftHSM2 in the tests); decision 222 says the production options are still to be researched and put to the owner, and nothing is chosen. No production key store is reachable from this repository, so the criterion waits for that decision.

**Notes (SEC-062, decision 205, lane w8, 2026-11-26):** The key custody record of SEC-062 (docs/adr/0156-security-3-backlog-batch.md, section SEC-062, accepted by decision 205) applies to the signing service. It resolves a signing key per tenant, with the operator's key as default; the seal's evidence names the key that sealed; a tenant key is used only through the signing service and is never exported; loading a tenant key is a tenant administration action that checks the certificate chain and its validity first, and the old reference stays readable so that earlier seals verify. The production key store stays open (decisions 221 to 223). This note changes no criterion and ticks none.

### FRW-053 Seal the final PDF with a time-stamp

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Relying party |

**Spec:** 12 §8.2 step 3, §6.1 (`finalisation.seal`), §14; ADR 0013 · **Requirements:** R13, R10 · **Depends on:** EXP-079, FRW-052, SPK-021

As a relying party, I want the final PDF sealed with a PAdES signature and an RFC 3161 time-stamp, so that any later change is detectable and the time is vouched for by a third party.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given two identical compiles, when the signing service seals, then the PDF carries a PAdES signature of the profile chosen by spike S8 and the workflow's `finalisation.seal`, and an RFC 3161 time-stamp token.
- [ ] Given the sealed sample, then it validates in Acrobat and in an independent validator with the seal intact.
- [x] Given any byte of the sealed PDF changed afterwards, then the validators report the signature as broken.
- [x] Given the seal certificate, then it is the operator's or the tenant's, not a certificate for any signer, and no text in the PDF says otherwise.
- [x] Given the time-stamping authority is unreachable, then no seal without a time-stamp is produced, the workflow stays `completed` and the operator is alerted.
- [x] Given the evidence times and the seal, then evidence times come from the server clock and the seal's time from the time-stamp token.

**Evidence (lane unblock, 2026-11-18, criteria 1, 3, 4 and 6; part of 5):** the seal step is `sealAndFinalise` (`services/document-service/src/workflow/seal-and-finalise.ts`) over the signing service (FRW-052's Evidence) and `finaliseSealed`. Criterion 1: `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-053.seal-and-finalise.test.ts` (18 tests: 9 on memory storage and 9 on PostgreSQL in Podman, run with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, none skipped; the key is in the test process, the authority is OpenSSL's `ts -reply`): a completed workflow is sealed at B-LT by default and at B-LTA when its `finalisation.seal` is `pades-b-lta`, the stored bytes begin with the input PDF, `openssl cms -verify` and `ts -verify` accept the signature and its time-stamp (and the document time-stamp), the DSS is present, the workflow reads `finalised`, and `GET /workflow/final` returns the same bytes. `frw-052-service.test.ts` (21 tests, `services/signing-service/test/`) covers the levels and refuses an unknown one, and the pair rule: the service seals only a PDF whose two compile hashes are its hash. The pair's hashes are the caller's claim (EXP-079 builds the pair; the local `lualatex` compiles of the sample differ byte for byte, which `frw-052-key-service.podman.test.ts` handles by refusing them as `pair_differs`). Criterion 3: in `frw-052-service.test.ts` and `frw-052-key-service.podman.test.ts` one flipped byte in the first byte, in the page content, in the middle of the first signed range and just after the signature (the second signed range) each make OpenSSL reject the signature (the spike's `spk-021-seal.test.ts` also flips the signature value and the validation data); the validator is OpenSSL (pyHanko was not run for this story). Criterion 4: the signature's `/Reason` names the operator or the tenant and says it is not the signature of any signer, there is no `/Name` or `/ContactInfo`, nothing in the seal says "signed by"; the seal's certificate is the one issued for the key of the operator or of the tenant (tested with two keys); the certificates are test certificates. Criterion 6: the outcome's seal time is the token's `genTime` (read back by OpenSSL, within a second of the service's answer, and within a minute of this machine's clock), `/M` and the audit times of the service are the service's own clock (tested with a clock set apart), and the transition's audit entry is stamped with the server clock given to the run, an hour from the token's time. Criterion 5, what is met: a time-stamp authority that does not answer gives no seal (`tsa_unreachable`, no bytes returned, `seal.failed` in the service's audit), the run is retried with the given delays, the workflow stays `completed`, nothing is stored, `/workflow/final` answers 404, `workflow.finalisation_failed` is audited and the alert port is called once; a later run seals it; an authority that fails twice and then answers is waited for with no alert; a refused request (the worker's credential) is not retried; an unreachable service is retried like an unreachable authority.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criterion 5):** the live path in `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-053.live-seal.test.ts` (see FRW-052's Evidence): with the time-stamp authority down, no seal is produced (the signing service's audit has no `seal.sealed`; nothing is stored), the workflow stays `completed`, the run is retried and then alerted (error log line, counter, audit entry), and a later run seals it. The Acrobat check (criterion 2) stays a person's.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, ADR 0420 proposed):** Criterion 5 is ticked on the live path. Nothing waits for Acrobat: criterion 2 stays unticked, and the files to open are `docs/spikes/2026-11-18-s8-sealing/sample-sealed-b-lt.pdf` and `sample-sealed-b-lta.pdf` (import `test-root-ca.cer` in the same folder for the trusted case; steps in the S8 report, section 6, and the lane unblock Notes). The final export job now carries the pin of a sealing export when the service seals, so the worker compiles twice (EXP-079); the compile itself was not run here (the job's handler is a stand-in that stores a sample PDF and the two equal hashes).

**Notes:** Criterion 5 is partly met at the gate: `finaliseWorkflow` refuses a seal without a time-stamp and an unreachable time-stamping authority, and keeps the workflow at `completed` (`packages/authority/test/workflow.finalisation.test.ts`). The operator alert is the workflow service's, so criterion 5 stays unticked. Criteria 1 to 4 need the signing service and the PDF and are not met. 12 §8.2 names baseline B-LT or B-LTA at sealing, while 12 §13 puts long-term validation in phase 4; see Open questions and FRW-056. S8's exit criterion fixes the profile. Second review (2026-11-06, SPK-027), SR-K8: the review recommends B-LT at sealing from phase 2 (validation data collected while the certificates are current), and B-LTA archive time-stamps with their renewal in phase 4 (FRW-056); owner to decide ([review](../review/2026-11-06-second-review.md)).

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, blocked):** No criterion is newly ticked. The owner's decisions 14 and 15 of 2026-11-15 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md) settle what the Notes above left open: the operator seals by default and a tenant's own key and certificate are used where the tenant supplies them (`selectSealingKey` already does this), and the seal level is PAdES B-LT in phase 2 (B-LTA is FRW-056, phase 4). Criteria 1 to 4 need a signing service with a key that cannot be exported, a certificate of the sealing party, a time-stamping authority and the validators (Acrobat, an independent validator); none is reachable from this repository, and the signing service (FRW-052) is itself blocked on the same things and on a new workspace package. A seal made with a key file on a worker, or a mock authority, would not meet them. Criterion 5 is met at the gate only: `finaliseWorkflow` refuses a seal without a time-stamp and an unreachable authority (`alert: true`, workflow stays `completed`); the operator's alert is not delivered by anything, because no alerting channel is specified (a failed run is audited as `workflow.finalisation_failed`). Criterion 6 needs a real time-stamp token whose time can be compared with the server clock.

**Notes (lane unblock, 2026-11-18, partial, ADR 0412 proposed):** Criteria 1, 3, 4 and 6 are ticked on the test chain, the SoftHSM2 or in-process key and the local OpenSSL time-stamp authority (owner decisions 221 to 223); nothing here is a production arrangement and no production library, key location, certificate or authority is chosen. Open: (2) Acrobat is a person's check. Open `E:\CodeBase\Svelte-RichText-Editor\.worktrees\unblock\docs\spikes\2026-11-18-s8-sealing\sample-sealed-b-lt.pdf` (and `sample-sealed-b-lta.pdf`); with the self-signed root untrusted Acrobat should say the signer's identity is unknown and not report the document as changed; after importing `test-root-ca.cer` as a trusted root it should report the signature valid, signed by "DocEdit Test Seal (SPK-021)", with an embedded time-stamp and long-term validation enabled (the S8 report, section 6, has the steps and the root's fingerprint). Those files are the spike's, made by `sealPdf` and not by the service; the service calls the same code. An independent validator other than OpenSSL (pyHanko, EU DSS) was not run on the service's output. (5) The refusal of a seal without a time-stamp, the unchanged `completed` state, the retry and the audit entry are tested; "the operator is alerted" is a call to a port (`OperatorAlert`) with no channel behind it, because the specification names none, so the criterion stays unticked. No job schedules a later run: a run that stops is started again by whoever calls `sealAndFinalise`. Open questions for the owner are in ADR 0412 (whose clock `/M` is, the retry policy, the alert channel). The final PDF is not yet sent by any live path: nothing calls `sealAndFinalise` after the final export job completes (FRW-049 and FRW-050 produce the PDF, and the two-compile pair of EXP-079 is not wired to the job), so no finalised workflow is produced in a running service. Lane w8 (2026-11-26): the SEC-062 note above on FRW-052 applies here too; nothing new is ticked by it.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-26, blocked):** Nothing new is ticked. Criterion 2 is the only one open: it needs Acrobat and an independent validator other than OpenSSL to accept the sealed sample with the seal intact. Owner decision 223 says the Acrobat validation criteria stay manual checks, so a person must open the sample (`docs/spikes/2026-11-18-s8-sealing/sample-sealed-b-lt.pdf`) and record the result. No other validator (pyHanko, EU DSS) is installed here, and adding one is a dependency this run does not allow.

### FRW-054 Store the sealed PDF immutably and anchor the chain head

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | M | must | Operator |

**Spec:** 12 §8.2 step 4, step 5, §6.2; 06 §4.2; 08 §12 · **Requirements:** R13, R4, R10 · **Depends on:** FRW-053, FRW-030, BE-003

As an operator, I want the sealed bytes stored immutably and the chain head written to write-once storage, so that the result cannot be replaced and its place in the history can be proved.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given sealing succeeds, then the sealed bytes are stored in write-once storage under the tenant, overwriting or deleting them is refused for the retention duration, and their hash is recorded.
- [x] Given the bytes are stored, then the workflow moves from `completed` to `finalised`, a control commit is written and `finalised` is raised; given the store fails, then the workflow stays `completed`.
- [x] Given the chain head at completion, then a signed, time-stamped head is written to write-once storage.
- [x] Given `GET /documents/{id}/workflow/final`, when an authorised caller asks, then it returns the sealed PDF (or a signed URL with an expiry) and its hash.
- [x] Given the sealed PDF, then no operation rewrites it.

**Evidence:** `services/document-service/test/objects/frw-054-sealed-store.test.ts` (6 tests, Node.js 22.18 against the Versity S3 Gateway of the test environment, in Podman; bucket with Object Lock created and removed by the test, name includes U-FRW-037-054): the sealed bytes are written under `objectKeys.final` for the tenant with Object Lock (`storeSealedPdf` in `services/document-service/src/objects/sealed-pdf.ts`), an overwrite (403) and a delete (403) are refused while the retention lasts, a second write for the same workflow is refused, and a store to a missing bucket gives `store_failed`, which `finaliseWorkflow` turns into `completed` with `raised` `['completed']` (criterion 2, at the gate). Criterion 1 end to end (fixer V-UX-031-045, V group 3 round 1, after the reviewer unticked it): `storeAndRecordSealedPdf` (`services/document-service/src/workflow/sealed-pdf-finalise.ts`) stores the sealed bytes, reads them back, hashes what was read and records that hash with `recordSealedPdf`; `services/document-service/test/postgres/frw-054.v-ux-031-045.store-and-record.pg.test.ts` (4 tests, run with `pnpm vitest run --project node` on that file on 2026-11-13, PostgreSQL and the Versity S3 Gateway in Podman, none skipped; own database and Object Lock bucket named with V-UX-031-045, removed afterwards) shows the record read back from PostgreSQL equals the hash of the original bytes and of the bytes read from the store, with the object key and retention end, that an overwrite and a delete are refused (403), and that nothing is recorded, and the gate stays at `completed`, when the store fails, when the stored bytes differ from the sealed hash, or when the record is refused. No workflow service calls it yet (criterion 2). The table test is `services/document-service/test/postgres/frw-054.v-frw-037-054.sealed-record.pg.test.ts` (5 tests, PostgreSQL in Podman, run with `pnpm vitest run` on that file; lane V-FRW-037-054): table `workflow_sealed_pdf` (`services/document-service/migrations/0061_workflow_sealed_pdf.sql`, `services/document-service/src/workflow/sealed-pdf-pg.ts`) records the hash of the stored bytes once per workflow, refuses a record whose stored hash differs from the sealed hash, keeps one tenant's record from another tenant's reads, and gives the service role no update and no delete. At that point criteria 2 and 5 stayed unticked (ticked since, see the lane w4 evidence below): no control commit recorded the hash or raises `finalised` (criterion 2), and no read endpoint exists (criterion 4).

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criteria 2, 4 and 5):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-054.finalise.test.ts` (10 tests: 5 on memory storage and 5 on PostgreSQL in Podman, run with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, none skipped; the real data plane with an owner and a participant connected) over `finaliseSealed` (`services/document-service/src/workflow/finalise-sealed.ts`), `SealedPdfStore` (`sealed-store.ts`) and the route `getWorkflowFinal` (`services/document-service/src/control-plane/workflow-routes.ts`). Criterion 2: from `completed`, sealed bytes with a time-stamp are stored and the workflow reads `finalised`; one control commit is added to the log and is the commit the outcome names; `workflow.transition` is audited with `from`, `to` and the sealed hash; `dep.workflow.finalised.v1` is raised with that `seq`; the document stays `locked` with `workflowActive` false; a store that fails leaves the state `completed`, adds no commit, raises nothing and gives `alert: true` with `store_failed`; a seal without a time-stamp and bytes that do not match the sealed hash are not finalised (no time-stamp is not stored at all); a finalised workflow is refused a second run.
