Criterion 4: the owner gets the PDF bytes with `Content-Type: application/pdf`, `ETag` `"sha256:<hex>"`, `Repr-Digest` (RFC 9530) of the hash, and 304 on `If-None-Match`; before `finalised` the answer is 404; a caller whose token names another document gets 403 or 404. Criterion 5: PUT, POST, PATCH and DELETE on the path answer 404 or 405, and the store refuses a second write for the workflow and keeps the first bytes. `services/document-service/test/postgres/frw-054.v-ux-031-045.store-and-record.pg.test.ts` gained a test of `ObjectSealedPdfStore` (the Versity S3 Gateway with Object Lock and PostgreSQL, Podman; 5 of 5 passed): the stored PDF and its record are read back, and a second store is refused. Overwrite and delete refusals by the object store remain proven by `frw-054-sealed-store.test.ts`.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criterion 3):** the signing service signs and time-stamps the head: `SigningService.anchor` and `POST /v1/anchor` (`services/signing-service/src/service.ts`, `http.ts`), tested by `services/signing-service/test/frw-054-anchor.test.ts` (9 tests, Node.js 22.18, OpenSSL, none skipped; the key in the test process, a local OpenSSL time-stamp authority): the signed record is the canonical text of the document, workflow, `seq` and head; `openssl cms -verify` accepts the detached signature over its SHA-256 and `openssl ts -verify` accepts the time-stamp token over the signature value; a different record or one flipped byte of the signature value is rejected; the tenant's own key signs when it has one; no credential, an unknown one and the export worker's own valid one are refused with no key use; one head is bound to a document and `seq`; an authority that is down gives no anchor (`tsa_unreachable`, audited); a failing audit sink gives no key call. The file is written once under the tenant by `ObjectAnchorStore` (`services/document-service/src/workflow/anchor-store.ts`, key `objectKeys.anchor`): `services/document-service/test/objects/frw-054-anchor-store.test.ts` (4 tests, the Versity S3 Gateway with Object Lock in Podman, none skipped) shows the file read back, the same record stored again as a no-op, another record for the workflow refused, and an overwrite and a delete refused (403) while the retention lasts. On the live path, `FinalSealer` anchors the head before it seals: `frw-053.live-seal.test.ts` (6 tests, memory and PostgreSQL) reads the stored file, checks its record against the job's pin (`completedSeq`, chain head), and verifies it with OpenSSL; with the authority down nothing is written and the run stops at stage `anchor` with the alert.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, partial):** Criteria 2, 4 and 5 are ticked; criterion 3 (the signed, time-stamped chain head) was unticked until the signing service existed and is ticked now (the Evidence above, ADR 0420 item 6). The hourly Merkle batch of 08 section 12 and the public log are not built. The sealed bytes that `finaliseSealed` takes are test bytes: nothing yet asks the signing service for them, so no live run reaches `finalised` (that is FRW-053's, blocked). Decisions the spec leaves open, recorded in docs/adr/0401-w4-FRW-054-finalise-and-final-route.md (proposed) for the owner: `finalised` keeps the lock of `completed`; the transition is anchored by an empty grants revision so that it is a control commit (`recordTransition` option `anchor`); the route is read with the head content's capability and answers the bytes, not a signed URL; the feature `seal` is not announced. The PostgreSQL composition has no object store, so `sealedPdfs` exists only in memory there and the route answers `not_found` on it. `lockFor('finalised')` is now decided, so `packages/authority/test/frw-029.workflow-lock.test.ts` and `frw-030.transition-record.test.ts` (the test that expected an error for `finalised`) were changed to match.

**Notes (earlier):** Lane U-FRW-037-054 (2026-11-13): criteria 1 and 2 are partly proven, at the store and the gate (see Evidence). Criterion 3 (signed, time-stamped chain head written once) is not built. Criterion 4 (`GET /documents/{id}/workflow/final`) waits for the document service's workflow routes, and the caller policy is open (Open questions). Who may call `/workflow/final` is not stated (Open questions). Criterion 2 is met at the gate only: `finaliseWorkflow` (`packages/authority/src/workflow/finalisation.ts`) keeps the workflow at `completed` when the store fails, and raises `finalised` only when the stored hash equals the sealed hash (`packages/authority/test/workflow.finalisation.test.ts`). The control commit and the write of `finalised` are the workflow service's, so criterion 2 stays unticked; criteria 1, 3, 4 and 5 are the store's and the service's (see ADR 0148).

### FRW-055 Export an evidence bundle for third-party verification

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Relying party |

**Spec:** 12 §8.2 step 4, §10; 08 §12; 11 §5 (exit criteria) · **Requirements:** R13, R4 · **Depends on:** FRW-041, FRW-053, FRW-054, HIS-002, HIS-033

As a relying party, I want an evidence bundle that I can verify without the operator's help, so that I can check the history and the seal for myself.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a finalised workflow, when an authorised caller requests `GET /documents/{id}/workflow/evidence`, then the response is a bundle holding the log segment, the control commits, the evidence record and verification instructions.
- [x] Given only the bundle and the sealed PDF, when a third party follows the instructions with the verification tool, then they can confirm the chain over the segment, that the chain-head hash in the certificate matches, that the PDF's signature and time-stamp are valid, and that the salted evidence hashes match the chain.
- [x] Given a bundle with one commit or one evidence item altered, then verification fails and names the item.
- [x] Given a segment with erased payloads (tombstones), then it still verifies.
- [x] Given the instructions, then they state what the evidence proves and what it does not, and make no claim of verified identity.
- [x] Given a retrieval, then it is audited and requires the credential the authorisation matrix assigns, because the bundle holds personal data.

**Evidence:** `packages/authority/test/workflow.bundle.test.ts`, "the bundle names every evidence item that does not verify" (criterion 3, for evidence items only: an altered item or commitment fails and is the one named; the commit half is the history service's, so the criterion stays open) and "the instructions state what the evidence proves and what it does not" (criterion 5). Source: `packages/authority/src/workflow/bundle.ts`, `packages/authority/src/workflow/language.ts`.

**Evidence (lane V-FRW-055-072, criteria 3 and 4, 2026-11-13):** `packages/history/test/frw-055.bundle-tamper.test.ts` (run with `pnpm vitest run packages/history/test/frw-055.bundle-tamper.test.ts packages/history/test/tombstone.test.ts`, 9 of 9 passed, Node.js): an altered commit payload and an altered stored hash are named by the log verifier (`altered`, `firstBadSeq`), an altered evidence item is named by the evidence verifier (`failed`), and a segment with two tombstoned payloads still verifies with no problems. Criterion 3 stays unticked: there is still no single command that runs both verifiers, and the log segment's chain verification is the history service's. Criterion 4 is ticked.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criteria 1 and 3):** the bundle is built by `buildWorkflowEvidenceBundle` (`services/document-service/src/workflow/evidence-bundle.ts`) and served by the route `getWorkflowEvidence` (`workflow-routes.ts`); the format and the verifier (`verifyWorkflowEvidenceBundle`) are `packages/history/src/workflow-evidence-bundle.ts`, run by `packages/history/scripts/verify-log.ts`. `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-055.evidence-bundle.test.ts` (4 tests: 2 on memory storage and 2 on PostgreSQL in Podman, run with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node` on that file, none skipped; the real data plane, an owner and a participant): a completed workflow's bundle holds the log from `sentSeq` to the head, the control commits with their payload (no steps) and no payload on any other record, the evidence items and the instructions; the retrieval is audited once; a participant gets 403 and no audit entry; before completion the answer is 404. The bundle verifies intact in the library and with the command (exit 0); with one control commit's metadata altered the verifier fails, names its `seq`, and the command exits 1; with one evidence item altered it names that item's identifier and no other, and the command exits 1. `packages/history/test/frw-055.workflow-bundle.test.ts` (7 tests, Node.js): the same on a built log, an altered payload of a control commit, a missing record, and a bundle of another format. The bundle was fetched for a `completed` workflow; no test reaches a live `finalised` one (the signing service is not built).

**Evidence (criterion 6, lane w4, 2026-11-26):** the authorisation matrix now has the route: row 10 (`manage`) of 07 §12 lists `GET /documents/{id}/workflow/evidence` and the operation `workflow.evidence` (`packages/authority/src/permissions/matrix.ts`, `model.ts`; the generated `spec/permissions/permissions-1.json`; 07 §12 and the route's entry in the unmatrixed list removed; `packages/authority/test/matrix.test.ts` passes). `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-055.evidence-bundle.test.ts`, the test for criterion 6 (6 tests in the file, memory and PostgreSQL, passed): the test reads the matrix and finds the route in the `manage` row with `workflow.evidence`; a document `editor` and a `viewer` get 403, no bundle and no audit entry; the owner gets the bundle twice and each retrieval is its own `workflow.evidence_retrieved` audit entry with an actor; a participant is refused as before.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criterion 2, not ticked):** three of the four checks exist and are tested. (a) The chain over the segment and (d) the salted evidence hashes against the chain: `verifyWorkflowEvidenceBundle` (`packages/history/src/workflow-evidence-bundle.ts`) now also requires each signing, completion and decline commitment to be the `evidenceHash` of a control commit of the segment, names one that is not, does not fail an item when a control payload is erased (`evidenceUnchecked`) and reports an anchor with no item (`unmatchedAnchors`); `packages/history/test/frw-055.evidence-in-chain.test.ts` (5 tests, Node.js). (c) The PDF's signature and time-stamp: `verifySealedPdf` and `verifyAnchorFile` (`services/signing-service/src/verify-sealed.ts`, built on `pkijs` and the roots the reader names, not on the sealing code), `services/signing-service/test/frw-055-verify-sealed.test.ts` (6 tests, none skipped; B-LTA seal accepted as OpenSSL accepts it, a flipped byte in a signed range or in the signature, a tail that is not validation data, an untrusted root and an unsigned PDF are reported). The command `services/signing-service/scripts/verify-final.ts <bundle> <sealed.pdf> --root <root>... [--anchor <file>]` runs all of it and the bundle's instructions name it: `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-053.live-seal.test.ts`, "a third party with only the bundle ..." (memory and PostgreSQL, none skipped) finalises a workflow on the live path, fetches the bundle from `GET /workflow/evidence`, runs the command on the files alone (exit 0, `intact`, the head of the signed anchor equals the bundle's chain at that `seq`), then with one byte of the PDF changed (exit 1, seal broken) and with one evidence item changed (exit 1, item named).

**Evidence (criterion 2, last check, lane w4, 2026-11-26):** the command now also reads the certificate of completion from the sealed PDF (`services/signing-service/src/certificate-head.ts`, `services/signing-service/scripts/verify-final.ts`; the text is read with `pdftotext` from poppler, which the reader installs or names with `--pdftotext`; no library was added). It finds the document, the workflow, `Completed at sequence number` and `Chain head at completion (SHA-256)` (a break inside the hash is allowed for) and requires the head to be the hash of the bundle's commit at that number; the report has `certificate: { status: matches | differs | absent | skipped, completedSeq, chainHead, bundleHead, problems }`, and `intact` needs `matches` (or `skipped` with `--skip-certificate`). Without the tool the command exits 3 and says how to go on. `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-053.live-seal.test.ts`, "a third party with only the bundle ..." (memory and PostgreSQL in Podman, 8 of 8 on PostgreSQL and 8 of 8 on memory, none skipped; one run of the whole file lost a connection to the load of the machine and passed when run again): the final export job now carries the certificate facts (`finalisation.certificate`), the stand-in compile draws them as text on a second page, the real signing service seals that PDF, and the command, run on the files alone, exits 0 with `certificate.status` `matches` and the head equal to the bundle's hash at the completion `seq`, with the chain, the evidence hashes, the signature, the time-stamp and the signed anchor checked as before. The sealed PDF of another workflow gives exit 1, `differs`, and names the head, the document and the workflow; a PDF with no certificate gives `absent`; `--skip-certificate` gives `skipped`; a missing tool gives exit 3. `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-055.certificate-text.test.ts` (3 tests): a real LuaTeX compile of the certificate pages (the export package's sandbox in Podman, house class) is read by the same functions and gives the document, the workflow, the sequence number and the head exactly. The instructions in the bundle (`packages/history/src/workflow-evidence-bundle.ts`) name the new check and the tool.

### FRW-056 Add long-term validation and renew time-stamps

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 4 | L | should | Operator |

**Spec:** 12 §8.2 step 5, §13, §14; ADR 0013 · **Requirements:** R13, R10 · **Depends on:** FRW-053, FRW-054, SPK-021

As an operator, I want long-term validation data added and time-stamps renewed on a schedule, so that a sealed PDF stays verifiable after certificates and time-stamps expire.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a sealed PDF, when the long-term validation job runs, then validation data is added only as an incremental update appended after the sealed bytes; the original byte range is unchanged and the original signature still validates.
- [x] Given a scheduled job, then it renews the time-stamp before the current one expires by appending a further time-stamp as an incremental update.
- [x] Given an update, then the originally sealed object stays stored byte for byte, the updated file is stored as a further object that begins with those bytes, and `/workflow/final` returns the latest.
- [ ] Given each update level, then Acrobat opens it and shows it valid, and the certification levels are tested.
- [x] Given a renewal fails, then the failure is visible to operators while time remains and the job is retried; running twice does not add two time-stamps for one expiry.

**Evidence (criterion 5, lane V-FRW-055-072, 2026-11-13):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-056.renewal-handler.pg.test.ts`, four tests on PostgreSQL in the shared Podman container (`pnpm exec vitest run services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-056.renewal-handler.pg.test.ts`: 4 passed, none skipped). The job queue runs the renewal through `JobRunner` (`services/document-service/src/control-plane/jobs/workflow-renewal.ts`, `renewalHandler`, which uses `planRenewal` and `applyRenewalResult`): one renewal for one expiry however often the job is enqueued, and the signer asked once; two runs racing for one expiry record one renewal (the compare and set on the expiry); a failed renewal is recorded on the job (`error.code` `renewal_failed`), the job is queued again and the second attempt renews, with the chain unchanged after the failure; no retry after the expiry, the job fails with `expired`. The operator alert is the failed job and its error, not a notification channel, which is not built. Review (round 2, V3 group 2): criterion 5 unticked. The compare and set that the test relies on is a table and an UPDATE written inside the test file, not product code, and the earlier note says the criterion holds only once the store enforces it atomically, which is not built. In the race test the signer is asked twice (both scripted answers are consumed) and only the recorded expiry is kept; in production the signer's call is what appends a time-stamp to the PDF, so two would be added. The handler needs a claim before the signer call (or an idempotent signer), and a real store. The handler and its four tests stand as evidence for the job wiring; tick criterion 5 when the store is the workflow service's and the signer is asked once per expiry. Fix (round 2, V3 group 2, 2026-11-13): criterion 5 ticked again. The handler now claims the expiry before it asks the signer (`RenewalStore.claim`, one atomic UPDATE), so only the claim's holder reaches the signer, and moves the expiry by a compare and set made by that holder (`swap`); a failed renewal releases the claim for the retry. The store is product code, `services/document-service/src/workflow/renewal-state-pg.ts` (`PgRenewalStore`), with migration `services/document-service/migrations/0068_frw_056_renewal_state.sql` (table `workflow_renewal_state`, row-level security, the test database gets it from the migrations). The same test file now holds six tests (`pnpm exec vitest run services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-056.renewal-handler.pg.test.ts`: 6 passed, none skipped, on PostgreSQL in the shared Podman container, 2026-11-13). The race test runs six handlers at once against one expiry, holds the winner at the signer until every claim has been answered, and asserts `signer.calls === 1` and code `stale` for each of the five losers, refused while the winner held the claim. Further tests: a failed renewal releases the claim and the retry asks again with the same idempotency key; a claim left by a run that died keeps the signer away until it lapses; another tenant can neither claim nor move the chain.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criteria 1 and 3):** the signing service extends a sealed PDF (`extendPdf` in `services/signing-service/src/pades.ts`; `SigningService.extend` and `POST /v1/extend`): fresh validation data as a new DSS when asked, then a further document time-stamp, both as incremental updates. `services/signing-service/test/frw-056-extend.test.ts` (5 tests, Node.js 22.18, OpenSSL, none skipped, a local OpenSSL time-stamp authority): the sealed bytes are the first bytes of the result, the original signature's `/Contents` is unchanged and validates (OpenSSL and `verifySealedPdf`), one DSS and one `DocTimeStamp` are added, a second renewal adds a second time-stamp over the first with the earlier bytes first, a repeat with the same key returns the first result and asks the authority once, an authority that is down gives no update (`tsa_unreachable`, audited) and a later request with the same key then works, and no credential, the export worker, a wrong hash, an empty key and a PDF with no signature are refused. The document service stores each update as a further object: `SealedPdfStore.storeUpdate` and `readLatest` (`services/document-service/src/workflow/sealed-store.ts`; memory and `ObjectSealedPdfStore`), `extendFinalised` (`extend-sealed.ts`) and the route `GET /workflow/final`, which now returns the latest. `services/document-service/test/objects/frw-056-sealed-updates.test.ts` (6 tests, Versity S3 Gateway with Object Lock in Podman, none skipped): the sealed object stays byte for byte, an update begins with the latest bytes, the same update is a no-op, a stale base and a file that is not an append are refused, update 10 is after update 9, and the sealed object and an update refuse an overwrite and a delete (403) while the retention lasts. `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-053.live-seal.test.ts`, the FRW-056 test (memory and PostgreSQL, none skipped): on a workflow finalised on the live path, `extendFinalised` stores update 1 (validation data and a time-stamp), `GET /workflow/final` returns it with its hash as the ETag, the sealed object read back is the original, OpenSSL and the verifier accept the signature and both time-stamps, the same key again stores nothing, a new key stores update 2 over update 1, and an authority that is down stores nothing.
**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-26, criterion 2):** `services/document-service/src/workflow/renewal-schedule.ts` (`scheduleRenewals`, `recordFirstExpiry`), `renewal-state-memory.ts`, `PgRenewalStore.start` (now idempotent) and `listDue`, the scheduled task `workflow.renewal.schedule` and the `workflow.renewal` runner in `src/server.ts`, and the settings `DOCEDIT_RENEWAL_LEAD_DAYS` and `DOCEDIT_RENEWAL_CLAIM_MS` (`config.ts`; both or neither, no default). The signing service now says when the seal's time-stamp ends (`expiresAtMs` in the `/v1/seal` answer, from the authority certificate's `notAfter`; `SealResult.tokenCertificates` in `services/signing-service/src/pades.ts`), `FinalSealer` records it at finalisation. `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-053.live-seal.test.ts`, "records the end of the seal time-stamp at finalisation ..." (memory and PostgreSQL, 2 of 2, none skipped; PostgreSQL in Podman, container `docedit-test-w4-postgres`, local OpenSSL time-stamp authority): the finalisation records the first expiry (a second start is refused), nothing is queued outside the lead time, one job `renewalJobId(doc, expiry)` is queued inside it however often the schedule runs, the renewal job through `sealedRenewalSigner` appends a document time-stamp as an update (`latest.n` 1, OpenSSL and `verifySealedPdf` accept the signature and the time-stamp), the expiry moves, and the document is not listed again. The test authority's certificate does not change, so the test's signer adds a year to the reported end, as a rotated certificate would. `frw-056.renewal-schedule.test.ts` (6 tests, memory, and PostgreSQL for `start` and `listDue`): one job per expiry across repeated and concurrent runs, a tenant's own documents only, no listing after the end, a failed renewal visible (`renewal_failed`, alert, retry) and then renewed, and the settings.

**Notes:** Pitfalls (12 §14): PDF signature validity after later changes; long-term validation. The seal profile is set by S8 (FRW-053). Lane authority-4, round 6: the renewal rules are a pure core in `packages/authority/src/workflow/renewal.ts` (`planRenewal`, `applyRenewalResult`), tested by `packages/authority/test/workflow.renewal.test.ts` (6 tests, fast-check). It covers the lead window (criterion 2 planning; the lead time is a parameter with no default, as the spec gives none), one time-stamp per expiry as a compare and set on the expiry, and a visible, retried failure while time remains (criterion 5). Not met: (1) and (3) the incremental update, the byte-for-byte prefix and `/workflow/final` are the export's and the store's; (2) the append itself and its Acrobat check need the PDF tooling and a browser-free viewer run; (4) the certification-level tests need the signing service; (5) the compare and set holds only once the store enforces it atomically, which is not built. Nothing ticked. Lane U-FRW-055-072, round 5 (2026-11-13): `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-056.renewal-queue.pg.test.ts` (PostgreSQL in Podman, 2 tests passed) shows that the job queue's `ifAbsent` enqueue keyed by document and expiry adds one renewal per expiry. That is the database's guarantee for one job, not the renewal: `planRenewal` is not wired to the queue, so criterion 5 stays open. Lane V-FRW-055-072, round 2 (2026-11-13): criterion 5 ticked (see its Evidence line); the renewal is wired to the queue. Its compare-and-set store is a PostgreSQL table made in the test only; the production store and its migration are the workflow service's and are not built. Criteria 1 to 4 are unchanged. Fix, round 2 (V3 group 2): the store and migration 0068 are now built (see the Evidence line). Open: a claim lapses after `claimMs` (a setting with no default; the spec gives none), and a run that asks after a lapse sends the same idempotency key (`renewalJobId` of document and expiry), so one time-stamp per expiry in that case also depends on the signing service honouring the key. That service is not built; its port `RenewalSigner` states the rule. The schema test `services/document-service/test/postgres/schema.pg.test.ts` lists every table by name and count and already failed before this lane's table was added (other lanes' tables); it now also lists `workflow_renewal_state` as unexpected.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-26, partial):** Criterion 2 is ticked (see the Evidence line of this date). Criterion 4 stays unticked: it asks for Acrobat to open each update level and show it valid, and for the certification levels to be tested; Acrobat is not on this machine and no independent validator opens the update levels either (OpenSSL and `verifySealedPdf` check the signature and the time-stamps, not Acrobat's own rules). The lead time and the claim time stay operator settings with no default (the spec gives none). One thing for the owner: renewing with the same authority certificate gives the same `notAfter`, and `applyRenewalResult` refuses an expiry that is not later (`invalid_timestamp`), so the schedule renews usefully only when the time-stamp authority has rotated its certificate before the old one ends; with one certificate until its end, the renewal job fails visibly (alert) and is retried. Whether the expiry should be the certificate's `notAfter` (as here) or the algorithm or key lifetime is a policy choice, not decided here.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-26, criterion 4 checked again):** Criterion 4 stays unticked and is not buildable here. Decision 223 of [owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md](../owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md) says the Acrobat validation criteria "stay manual checks", and the seal certificate and time-stamp authority are a self-signed test pair for now, which Acrobat would show as untrusted whatever the update level is. What is left is a person opening each update level of a sealed file in Acrobat with a trusted production certificate, and testing the certification levels (DocMDP permissions) there; the repository has no DocMDP code, and the spec names the check as one made in Acrobat (12 §14). Everything else of the story is ticked.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, partial, later):** Criteria 1 and 3 are ticked at the operation: `extendFinalised` is the body of the long-term validation job and of the renewal (`sealedRenewalSigner` implements the renewal job's `RenewalSigner`). Nothing schedules them, so criterion 2 stays unticked: no task finds the workflows whose newest time-stamp expires soon and enqueues `workflow.renewal`, the renewal job is not registered in the service, and the initial expiry state is not written at finalisation (the signing service returns `expiresAtMs`, the authority certificate's `notAfter`, as the new expiry). The lead time and the claim time stay settings with no default. Criterion 4 is a person's: open the sealed PDF after an update in Acrobat. A file to open is made by the test above; none is kept in the repository (`sample-sealed-b-lta.pdf` in `docs/spikes/2026-11-18-s8-sealing/` has a document time-stamp but no later update). The update's retention uses the same setting as the sealed PDF (`DOCEDIT_SEAL_RETENTION_DAYS`). The signing service keeps the results of served idempotency keys in memory, so a restart forgets them; a repeat after a restart asks the authority again and the store takes the newer bytes only if it is based on the latest.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, blocked, earlier):** No criterion is newly ticked; criterion 5 stays ticked as before. Owner decision 15 of 2026-11-15 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md) sets the levels: PAdES B-LT in phase 2 and B-LTA in phase 4, so this story is the B-LTA step (an archive time-stamp and its renewal). What stops each criterion. Criteria 1 and 2 need a sealed PDF made by the signing service, a timestamp authority and a validator to show that the original signature still validates after the update: FRW-052 and FRW-053 are blocked on those (their Notes), and no PDF signing or validation tool is present here (no `qpdf`, no `pdfsig`, no Acrobat; the repository holds `pdf-lib` and PDF.js, which do not validate signatures). A byte appender over a test file would show the prefix property only, and would not show that a signature still validates or that the added data is validation data. Criterion 3 would need the update producer of criteria 1 and 2, a second write-once object per update and a table row for it (a migration and the schema test), and `GET /workflow/final` returning the latest one: the route returns the one sealed object today (ADR 0401), and building the storage for updates that nothing can yet produce would be scaffolding. Criterion 4 needs Acrobat and the certification levels, neither of which is reachable. The pure renewal rules and the job (`packages/authority/src/workflow/renewal.ts`, `services/document-service/src/control-plane/jobs/workflow-renewal.ts`, `PgRenewalStore`) stand as they were. Nothing new was built for this story.

### FRW-057 Show visible signature appearances at the field positions

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 3 | M | could | Relying party |

**Spec:** 12 §12 (PDF), §13 (phase 3); 09 §3.1, §7.1, §9.3 (rule 7) · **Requirements:** R13, R5 · **Depends on:** FRW-053, EXP-058

As a relying party, I want visible signature appearances placed where the signature fields are on the page, so that the sealed PDF shows who signed where.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a PDF export of a document with fields, then `layout.json` holds an entry for every field with its identifier, key, page and rectangle.
- [x] Given the rectangles, when a document is sealed, then the signing service draws a visible appearance inside the rectangle of each signature and initials field.
- [x] Given `layout.json`, then it is validated against its schema before use, and a rectangle outside the page bounds is not used.
- [x] Given visible appearances are added, then the two-compile comparison of EXP-079 is unaffected, because the appearances are added after the compile.

- [ ] Given the appearance, then the sealed PDF still validates in Acrobat and in an independent validator.
- [x] Given a field with no rectangle in `layout.json`, then the seal is still applied and the missing appearance is reported as a diagnostic.

**Evidence:** none ticked. Review (round 3): `packages/authority/test/workflow.appearance.test.ts` shows that `planAppearances` reports a missing rectangle as a diagnostic (the second half of the last criterion), but "the seal is still applied" is asserted only against the constant `sealApplies: true`, and nothing calls the planner. The criterion stays open until the signing service applies the seal. Fix (round 3): the constant `sealApplies` field is removed from `AppearancePlan`; the tests now cover only what the planner does: "gives each field with a rectangle its appearance and each field without one a diagnostic (property)" (300 runs over 8 fields) and "never throws and draws only signature and initials fields, whatever the layout holds (property)" (each wanted field is drawn or reported, never both, never neither). `planAppearances` and `validateLayout` are exported from `packages/authority/src/index.ts`, and the test imports them from there.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criteria 2, 4 and 6):** the signing service draws the appearances: `appendAppearances` and `sealPdf`'s `appearances` option (`services/signing-service/src/pades.ts`), `planFieldAppearances` (`service.ts`: `validateLayout`, the twips to points conversion from the top-left, `planAppearances` against the page boxes of the compiled PDF) and `PdfDocument.pageList` (`pdf.ts`). `services/signing-service/test/frw-057-appearances.test.ts` (5 tests, Node.js 22.18, OpenSSL, none skipped): a stamp annotation with its own appearance stream is drawn inside the rectangle of the signature field and of the initials field and not for a text field (`/Rect [20 20 120 50]`, `(Electronically signed) Tj`); the sealed bytes begin with the input, and the appearances come after it and before the signature; OpenSSL and `verifySealedPdf` accept the seal, and one flipped byte in an appearance breaks it (the signature covers the appearances). Criterion 4: appearances are added in the signing service to the bytes that the equal pair of compiles agreed on; a pair that names the bytes with appearances is refused as before (`pair_differs`), and `FinalSealer` asks for the appearances only after the pair is read. Criterion 6: a field with no rectangle, an entry that is not valid (`entry_invalid`), a page that does not exist (`unknown_page`) and a rectangle outside its page (`rect_outside_page`) are reported in the answer, the seal is applied for the others and the signature validates. On the live path (`services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-053.live-seal.test.ts`, "draws the appearances ...", memory and PostgreSQL, none skipped): the document's signature and initials fields and the job's `layout.json` give the request (`services/document-service/src/workflow/appearances.ts`; `appearances.test.ts`, 5 tests, takes the lines from the signing evidence, uses no layout in another unit or of another `seq`), the stamp is in the stored sealed PDF at the rectangle, the seal validates, and the initials field with no rectangle is in the audit entry `workflow.appearance_diagnostics`.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-26, criterion 3):** the whole file is checked before use. `validateLayoutFile` (`packages/authority/src/workflow/layout-file.ts`, moved from the export worker, whose `validateLayout` now calls it, so there is one copy of the rules) checks every member of the schema; `appearanceRequestFor` (`services/document-service/src/workflow/appearances.ts`) refuses a file that fails any check and then takes no rectangle from it, and `validateLayout` and the signing service still drop a rectangle outside its page. `packages/authority/test/frw-057.layout-file.test.ts` (4 tests, 15 cases, Node.js 22.18) and `services/document-service/src/workflow/appearances.test.ts`, "validates the whole file against its schema before use" (8 bad files, a mixed file and the good one). The export worker's `layout.test.ts` and `exp-058-fields.test.ts` and the live path (`frw-053.live-seal.test.ts`, memory and PostgreSQL, none skipped) still pass. The test layouts now have the pages the schema requires (a field on page 1 of a file with no pages was accepted before and is refused now).

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-26, checked again):** No criterion changed. Criterion 1: the owner's decisions 196 to 220 do not cover the layout key (decision 220 names the spike choices S3 and S6, ADRs 0020 and 0030 to 0052 and protocol decisions D40 to D119), so the choice between rewording the criterion and adding `key` to the published schema is still the owner's; the options and the recommendation (reword; the spec's default, 09 §7.1) are now in the proposed [ADR 0435](../adr/0435-w4-FRW-057-layout-field-key.md). Criterion 5: decision 223 keeps the Acrobat check manual, and the seal certificate is a self-signed test certificate that Acrobat would not trust; the independent half (OpenSSL and `verifySealedPdf` accept the sealed file with appearances) is tested, but the criterion asks for both, so it stays unticked.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-26, partial):** Criterion 3 ticked. Criterion 5 stays a manual check by the owner's decision 223 of 2026-11-18 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md: "The Acrobat validation criteria stay manual checks"). Criterion 1 stays unticked: the owner has not answered the rewording proposed below, and the alternative, adding `key` to each `fields` entry, changes the published schema of 09 §7.1 and the converter's map (`fields` of `map.json` carries identifiers only) in the export lane. The signing request joins the key from the document by the identifier. `LAYOUT_SCHEMA` itself stays in the export worker; the validator that mirrors it is the authority's, so a change to the schema is a change to both files (the export worker's layout tests exercise the validator through `validateLayout`).

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, partial, later):** Criteria 2, 4 and 6 are ticked. Open, and why. Criterion 1: an entry of `layout.json` has no `key`, and the published schema (09 section 7.1) refuses members it does not list; the signing request joins the key from the document by the identifier. Proposed rewording for the owner, as in the earlier note: "an entry for every field with its identifier, page and rectangle; the key is read from the document by the identifier". Criterion 3: the `fields` that are used are validated by `validateLayout` in the document service and again in the signing service, the file's unit and `seq` are checked, and a rectangle outside the page is not used; the whole file is validated against `LAYOUT_SCHEMA` only by the export when it writes it, because that schema lives in the export worker and the document service has no copy (no schema file is published under `spec/`). Criterion 5: Acrobat is a person's check; the file to open is made by the live test above (the stored sealed PDF), and none is kept in the repository. What an appearance shows is a proposal: `Electronically signed`, the participant by name (else identifier) and the UTC date of the signing, or `Not signed`; no claim of identity (FRW-058). Text is drawn in Helvetica without embedding; a character outside Latin-1 is drawn as `?`. A signature field that was never signed still gets a `Not signed` box. Open question for the owner: whether the box should belong to the participant (an image they adopted) and not to the seal; no image is drawn.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, blocked, earlier):** Nothing is newly ticked. What exists: the export writes `fields` in `layout.json`, keyed by field identifier, each with `page` and `rect` in twentieths of a point from the top-left corner of the page, and checks the whole file with `validateLayout` of `services/export-worker/src/layout.ts` against `LAYOUT_SCHEMA` (EXP-058, `packages/export-latex/src/exp-058-fields.compile.test.ts`; `services/export-worker/src/exp-058-fields.test.ts`). What stops each criterion. Criterion 1: an entry has no `key`, and the schema refuses members it does not list (`additionalProperties: false`), so adding one is a change to the published schema of 09 §7.1, which is not this story's to make silently; the signing service holds the document and can join the key from the identifier. Proposed rewording for the owner: "an entry for every field with its identifier, page and rectangle; the key is read from the document by the identifier". Criteria 2, 4 and 5 need the signing service, a sealing key, the compiled PDF and the validators (Acrobat and an independent one): FRW-052 and FRW-053 are blocked on those (their Notes), and a drawn appearance without them would be a mock. Criterion 3: the rectangle clause is met by `planAppearances` and the file is validated by the export when it writes it, but the check "before use" belongs to the service that reads the file, which is not built, so it stays unticked as before. Criterion 6: the diagnostic for a missing rectangle is met by the planner; "the seal is still applied" needs the signing service. One thing the signing service must do and the planner does not say: the planner's rectangles are `[x0, y0, x1, y1]` with `x0 < x1` and `y0 < y1` in the units of the page bounds it is given, while `layout.json` is in twips from the top-left corner and a PDF's own user space is in points from the bottom-left, so the service converts (divide by 20, flip the vertical axis with the page height) before it calls the planner. Earlier notes: Criterion 3 is met for its rectangle clause only: `validateLayout` checks the `fields` part of `layout.json` and drops a bad entry; the whole file is validated against its schema by the export (09 §9.3), which is not built here, so the schema clause stays unticked (`packages/authority/test/workflow.appearance.test.ts`). Criteria 1, 2, 4 and 5 are the export's and the signing service's. Phase 3 by 12 §13. What an appearance shows (the adopted image, the name, the date) and whether it belongs to each participant or to the seal is not specified (Open questions). `layout.json` precision depends on spike S4.

## Legal stance (12 §8.3)

### FRW-058 Keep signing language within the legal stance

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | S | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 12 §8.3; ADR 0013; PRODUCT (brand commitments); 11 §9 (risk 15) · **Requirements:** R13 · **Depends on:** none

As a maintainer, I want a check that no interface text, document, certificate or marketing copy over-claims what a signature means, so that the product never says more than a simple electronic signature with evidence and a seal.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given all interface strings, documentation, API descriptions, the certificate template and marketing copy in the repository, when CI runs the wording check, then none contains "qualified" in the sense of a signature level, "legally binding everywhere" or similar phrases from a maintained list, and a match fails the build.
- [x] Given the product describes what it provides natively, then the text says: a simple electronic signature with an audit trail and a tamper-evident seal applied by the operator or by the tenant's own key.
- [x] Given the integration guide, then it states that advanced and qualified signatures are reached through a `SignatureProvider` adapter, and that whether a use is valid in a jurisdiction is a legal question for the operator and its customers.
- [x] Given the documentation, then it states how the ceremony and the evidence record address intent, consent and retention (ESIGN and UETA) without claiming validity.
- [x] Given any text that mentions a participant's authentication, then it says "as asserted by the host" and never "verified".

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-17):** `packages/authority/test/frw-058.integration-guide.test.ts` (vitest, Node.js 22.18, 5 of 5 passing): the signing section of `apps/reference-host/docs/integration-guide.md` ("Signing and identity (FRW-058; 12 section 8.3)") carries the native description of `NATIVE_SIGNING_DESCRIPTION` (criterion 2), names `SignatureProvider`, the hash-only send and the operator's legal question (criterion 3), names ESIGN and UETA with intent, consent and retention and no validity claim (criterion 4), says "as asserted by the host" with no "verified" participant wording (criterion 5), and `findOverclaims` finds no phrase of the list in it. Criterion 1 not run here, see Notes.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-26, criterion 1):** `packages/authority/test/frw-058.repository-wording.test.ts` (3 tests, Node.js 22.18, none skipped; with `tests/security/test/language-check.test.ts` of SEC-043, 11 of 11 passing): it scans the text files under packages, services, apps, infra, `spec/` (the API descriptions), the docs that are not reviewed records, `PRODUCT.md` and `README.md` for every phrase of `OVERCLAIM_PHRASES` (the maintained list of this story), ignoring negated lines, test sources and the reviewed records, and fails on a match. It runs under `pnpm test`, which `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs, so a match fails the build; no root configuration was changed. The control test shows each phrase of the list is flagged, and a scan of fewer than 100 files fails. The scan found one phrase, "verified identity" in a comment of `services/document-service/src/data-plane/asserted-claims.ts` (it meant the token's identity record, not a person); the comment is reworded. Owner decision 99 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md) settles the open term: the specification may use "qualified" for signature levels, and the check covers interface texts and outward-facing documents, which is the scope above.

**Notes:** Phase inferred (it must hold before the first signing language ships, which is phase 2). The 12 §8.3 sentence about how Microsoft and others describe comparable features is background, not copy. Counsel review is FRW-087. Authority-4 (2026-11-13): `findOverclaims` and `isWithinLegalStance` (`packages/authority/src/workflow/language.ts`, tested by `packages/authority/test/workflow.language.test.ts`) check text against a maintained phrase list, and the native description and the "as asserted by the host" phrase are constants there. Criterion 1 (lane w4, 2026-11-26): ticked. The term is decided (owner decision 99) and the check runs inside `pnpm test`, which CI already runs, so no root configuration was needed. Earlier note, now stale: (1) the repository-wide CI run needs root configuration (`.github/`, `package.json`), which is the main session's, and a decision on existing spec text that uses "qualified" as a term (ADR 0148 round 4); the owner has not decided that term, so the check is not wired into CI. Lane w4 (2026-11-17): criteria 2 to 5 ticked by adding the signing section to the integration guide, which had no such text. Criterion 3 asks the guide to name "advanced and qualified" signatures; the phrase list matches "qualified signature" as a substring, so the guide says "the advanced and the qualified levels" instead, which keeps the same meaning and passes the list. The guide's wording is a proposal written here from spec 12 section 8.3; the owner has not reviewed it, and counsel review (FRW-087) is still open. Criterion 4 describes what the ceremony and the evidence record are designed to address; it does not show that the ceremony records intent or consent in the code.

### FRW-059 Sign through a signature provider

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 4 | L | could | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 12 §8.3, §13 (phase 4), §1.1; ADR 0013 · **Requirements:** R13, R6 · **Depends on:** FRW-053, PRO-091, PRO-092

As an integrating developer, I want a `SignatureProvider` adapter that signs document hashes remotely, so that a deployment can reach advanced or qualified signatures through a provider without the platform becoming a trust service provider.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a `SignatureProvider` interface with the Cloud Signature Consortium API as its target, when a workflow is configured to use it, then at finalisation only the document hash is sent to the provider and the returned signature is embedded in the PDF.
- [x] Given a workflow without a provider, then the native seal of FRW-053 is used unchanged.
- [ ] Given the provider, then the signature level and signer identification it reports are recorded in the evidence as asserted by the provider, and the platform's language still follows FRW-058.
- [x] Given a provider failure, then the workflow stays `completed`, the failure is visible, and nothing is half-signed.
- [x] Given a mock provider, then contract tests for the adapter pass.

**Evidence:** `packages/authority/test/workflow.provider.test.ts`, "the signature provider contract (FRW-059 criterion 5)": five adapter behaviours (signs with a time-stamp, unreachable, refuses, invalid response, throws) run against the mock through `sealWithProvider`, and the seal succeeds exactly when it should. Criterion 4, "a provider failure", is tested in "a provider failure leaves nothing half-signed and visible": no sealed outcome, no asserted facts and no embedding after any failure, and `finaliseWorkflow` stops at the seal with `completed`, `alert` true and `signing_unavailable`. Source: `packages/authority/src/workflow/provider.ts`.

**Notes:** `SignatureProvider` is not in the port list of 05 §8.3; adding it is a PRO change. Native advanced or qualified signatures, identity verification and notary roles stay out of scope (12 §13). Authority-4 (2026-11-13): the adapter and its contract are `packages/authority/src/workflow/provider.ts`, proposed in ADR 0148 round 4 as a PRO-level port, not yet in 05 §8.3. Not met: (1) the PDF embedding of the returned signature and the hash-only check on the wire (the request type carries the hash alone, and the test shows it); (2) the native seal is the signing service's, and `sealWorkflow` only chooses it without a provider; (3) the asserted facts are returned by `sealWithProvider` as `assertedBy: 'provider'`, but the evidence tables that record them are the workflow service's. (4) and (5) are ticked above.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-18, blocked):** No criterion is newly ticked; criteria 4 and 5 stay ticked as before. Owner decision 15 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md) sets the native seal level and decision 99 allows the word "qualified" to name signature levels in the specification. (The rest of this earlier note was cut off in an edit; its reasons are stale and are replaced by the note of 2026-11-26 below.)

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-26, criteria 1 and 2):** the provider is reached through the Cloud Signature Consortium API v2. `services/signing-service/src/csc.ts`: `CscKeyService` implements the `KeyService` port over `credentials/info`, `credentials/authorize` (explicit mode) and `signatures/signHash` (ADR 0411 named this fit), `cscCertificates` gives the provider's chain to the service, and the provider's signature is checked against the certificate's key before it is used. Only the SHA-256 of the CMS signed attributes (which holds the digest of the PDF's byte ranges) is sent, with the credential identifier and the access token; the PDF, the document identifier and the tenant never leave the service. `services/signing-service/test/frw-059-csc.test.ts` (5 tests, Node.js 22.18, OpenSSL, a test double of a CSC provider in `test/support/csc-server.ts`, none skipped): the provider receives exactly `credentials/info` and `signatures/signHash`, one 32-byte hash, no PDF bytes and no document or tenant identifier; the signature in the sealed PDF (B-LTA) verifies with OpenSSL against the provider's certificate and with `verifySealedPdf`; a tenant with no provider is sealed by the operator's key with no call to any provider (criterion 2); the explicit authorisation mode works, `oauth2code` and a non-SHA-256 digest are refused; down, refusing, garbled, wrong-key and disabled-key providers give no sealed bytes. Live path: `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-053.live-seal.test.ts`, "finalises with the signature of a provider reached through the CSC API ..." (memory and PostgreSQL, none skipped): a completed workflow is finalised with the provider's signature, the sealed PDF validates, the provider saw only hashes and no document identifier, and a provider that is down leaves the workflow `completed` with the operator alert and nothing stored.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-26, partial):** Criteria 1 and 2 ticked; criterion 4 and 5 stay ticked, now also through the live path. Criterion 3 stays unticked. What exists: the signing service returns what the provider asserts (`provider` in the `/v1/seal` answer: name, level, signer, from the credential's subject and the level the operator records from the provider's own statement), and the document service writes the audit entry `workflow.seal_provider_asserted` with `assertedBy: 'provider'` (tested in the live test above). What is missing: the participant evidence ledger (`workflow_evidence`, items `viewed`, `signed`, ...) and the evidence bundle of FRW-055 hold per-participant items committed in the chain, and a seal-level item would need a new item kind and a control commit; that is a design change to 12 §7 and §8.2, not made here. Whether the audit entry is enough "evidence" is the owner's word. Two proposals, not decided: (1) the provider is chosen per tenant, by the signing service's key directory (`tenantKeyIds`), and not per workflow: a `finalisation` member that names a provider would change 12 §6.1; (2) the level is a string the operator records, because CSC `credentials/info` has no level member; the `description` of the credential is the fallback. `SignatureProvider` is still not in the port list of 05 §8.3 (a PRO change), and `provider.ts` in `@docedit/authority` stays the pure model of the provider's answer; the working port is `KeyService`. Not done: the `oauth2code` mode (the signer authorises at the provider's own page), the access token's own OAuth 2 flow (the deployment supplies it), the per-signer certificate of an advanced or qualified signature (the provider's credential here is one the tenant owns), and any run against a real provider (none is available; the double is not a copy of one).

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-26, checked again):** `docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md` has no decision on FRW-059, `SignatureProvider` or where a provider's assertion is recorded (decisions 15 and 99 cover the native seal level and the word "qualified" only). Criterion 3 stays unticked because the evidence ledger (12 §7) is per participant, with items fixed by `applicableItems`, and the bundle of FRW-055 holds only that ledger and the log segment: a seal-level item would add a participant kind or an item kind and a control commit, which changes 12 §7 and §8.2. That is a design change for the owner, not a default the spec names. Until then the assertion is in the audit log as `workflow.seal_provider_asserted` with `assertedBy: 'provider'` (tested). Status: partial, unchanged.

## Templates, prefill and bulk send (12 §9)

### FRW-060 Read fields and field values

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 12 §9, §10 (control plane), §3.2 (`key`); 05 §6.1; 07 §8 · **Requirements:** R13, R6 · **Depends on:** FRW-003, FRW-007, PRO-040, PRM-041

As an integrating developer, I want to list a document's fields and read their values by key, so that my application can extract the answers.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `GET /documents/{id}/fields`, then it returns the derived list of fields (identifier, `key`, `kind`, `label`, `assignee`, `required`, `readOnly`) as `{ items, nextCursor }` with an `ETag`, and a `limit` of at most 200.
- [x] Given `GET /documents/{id}/field-values`, then it returns the current values by key, as stored (decimal strings stay strings).
- [x] Given `GET /documents/{id}/field-values?seq=N`, then it returns the values as they were at `seq` N, and the response is immutable and cacheable like a pinned content read.
- [x] Given a `seq` below the caller's `minVisibleSeq`, then the request is refused as for content at a `seq`.
- [x] Given a key that does not exist, then the request fails with `not_found`.
- [x] Given a caller who cannot view the document, then the request is refused with `forbidden`; values the caller may not read (ADR 0019) are redacted, and every other value is returned.

**Evidence:** `services/document-service/src/control-plane/fields.test.ts` (memory store; the PostgreSQL run skips without a database). "lists the derived fields as a cursor list with an ETag, at most 200 a page" (criterion 1: eight fields, two a page to the end, `limit` 201 refused); "returns the values by key as stored, decimal strings unchanged, and filters by key" (criterion 2); "answers not_found for a key that does not exist" (criterion 5); "serves a pinned read at the head as immutable, answers 304 to a repeat, and refuses a seq above the head" (the head and `seq` above it only); "refuses a caller who cannot view the document with forbidden" (criterion 6, refusal part). Criteria 4 and 6 (redaction part): `services/document-service/test/control-plane/field-values-gate.test.ts` (memory; PostgreSQL skips without a database; 2026-11-13, passes): "redacts a value the caller may not read and returns every other value" (a non-owner reads `{ redacted: true }` for the hidden field and the number unchanged, the owner reads both, a key read stays redacted) and "refuses a seq below the caller gate as forbidden, and serves the head and the gate above it" (one commit after creation, a viewer of an editors-only document gets 403 at seq 0 and 200 at the head). Criterion 3: `services/document-service/src/control-plane/field-values-seq.test.ts` (memory; PostgreSQL skips without a database; 2026-11-13, passes): a commit with the prefill steps of a text field is appended to the log; the head then shows the new value, and `?seq=` at the earlier seq still shows the old one, twice identically, with `private, max-age=31536000, immutable` and a 304 to its ETag.

**Notes:** Packet C (2026-11-13, round 9): criterion 3 is ticked on `field-values-seq.test.ts`, which pins a read before a value change. Lane FRW-053-065 (2026-11-13): criteria 4 and 6 are ticked on `test/control-plane/field-values-gate.test.ts`, which hides a value by field identifier (the ADR 0019 value class). Hiding a whole region, and the omission of fields inside it from the list, is not tested here. The capability these resources require is not in the authorisation matrix (07 §12); `view` is assumed here and must be confirmed. The values are not a second source of truth: they are read from the tree. Cross-check: reads pinned to a sequence number pass the history-visibility gate of PRM-042 from phase 2; phase 1b serves the head.

### FRW-061 Prefill field values by key

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 12 §9, §10, §14; 04 §10; 06 §2.2; 05 §6.1 · **Requirements:** R13, R10 · **Depends on:** FRW-003, FRW-009, FRW-060, SYN-030

As an integrating developer, I want to set field values by key before a document is sent, so that known data is already in the document when the participant opens it.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `PATCH /documents/{id}/field-values` with keys and values, when every key and value is valid, then one server-authored commit with origin `prefill` sets them, attributed to the user named in `DEP-Actor` or to `system`.
- [x] Given a key of kind `signature`, `initials`, `dateSigned` or `formula`, then the request is refused and nothing is applied.
- [x] Given an unknown key or a wrongly typed value, then the request is refused with `field_invalid`, the problem names the key, and nothing is applied (the request is atomic).
- [x] Given a value that contains TeX control sequences, markup or spreadsheet formula characters, then it is stored as typed data and reaches the PDF only through the same emitters and escaping layer as any other text.
- [x] Given the document is not open in a room, then a transient authority loads it, applies the commit, stores it and exits, without subscribers or timers.
- [x] Given the same request is repeated with the same `Idempotency-Key`, then one commit is written.
- [x] Given a participant's fill commit based before the prefill and changing the same field, then it is rejected with `conflict` (FRW-010).

**Evidence:** `services/document-service/src/control-plane/fields.test.ts` (memory store). "refuses a prefill of a signature field, and of a field the document does not have, and applies nothing" (criterion 2: signature, initials, dateSigned and formula each refused by key; criterion 3: `field_invalid` naming `/values/<key>` for an unknown key and for a number that is not a decimal, a choice outside its options, and a mixed request applied not at all); "requires If-Match on a prefill and refuses an empty prefill" (guard rails).

**Evidence (lane U-FRW-055-072, 2026-11-13):** criterion 1 on `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-061.prefill-applied.test.ts`, run against PostgreSQL in Podman and against memory storage with the real data plane (`realDataPlane: true`, not the stub), both pass: the prefill answers 200 with the stored values, the commit log holds one `prefill` commit attributed to `system` (no DEP-Actor header is sent), and `?seq=` before it still shows the old values. Review (round 1, U3 group 2): criterion 1 unticked. The branch "attributed to the user named in `DEP-Actor`" has no test; tick it when a prefill with a `DEP-Actor` header is shown to give a commit by that user. Fix (round 1, U3 group 2, 2026-11-13): criterion 1 re-ticked. The same file adds "a prefill sent with DEP-Actor is one prefill commit attributed to the user it names": a service credential sends the PATCH with a `DEP-Actor` sub unique to the run, the answer is 200, and the commit log holds one `prefill` commit whose `author.user` is that sub (not `system`; the pipeline resolved the sub to a surrogate, which the log answers as the sub). `pnpm exec vitest run services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-061.prefill-applied.test.ts`: 4 of 4 passed (2 cases on memory storage, 2 on PostgreSQL in the shared Podman container), none skipped.

**Evidence (lane U-FRW-055-072, round 2, 2026-11-13):** criterion 5 on `services/document-service/test/data-plane/frw-061.transient-prefill.test.ts` (memory stores with a `setTimeout` spy, and PostgreSQL in the shared Podman container; `pnpm exec vitest run services/document-service/test/data-plane/frw-061.transient-prefill.test.ts`: 2 of 2 passed, none skipped): a prefill applied by `host.apply` on a document with no room open stores one commit at seq 1 with origin `prefill`, leaves no room and no transient run, and the memory run sets no timer (the PostgreSQL run does not check timers, because the driver's pool keeps its own). `host.apply` is the call the outbox makes (`data-plane/outbox.ts`). Criterion 7 on `packages/authority/test/permissions.room.test.ts`, "rejects a fill after a server-authored change of the same field (FRW-010)": a prefill-origin commit at seq 1, then a fill made on base 0 of the same field is refused with `conflict` (Node, `pnpm exec vitest run packages/authority/test/permissions.room.test.ts -t "server-authored change of the same field"`, 1 passed). That test drives the room directly, not the HTTP route.

**Evidence (lane w4, second pass, 2026-11-18, criteria 4 and 6):** Criterion 6: `prefillFieldValues` now carries `idempotencyKey: true` in `packages/protocol/src/definition/frw.ts` (owner decision 184: the key on the prefill route is part of ADR 0342; `pnpm generate` rewrote `spec/dep/dep-1.*.json`; 05 §6.1 names the route). `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-061.prefill-idempotent.test.ts` and `frw-061.prefill-hostile.test.ts` (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node`, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman project `docedit-test-w4`, 4 and 4 tests, passed): the same request sent twice with one `Idempotency-Key` gets the first answer back with a stale `If-Match`, and the log holds one `prefill` commit; the same body under another key is 412 `precondition_failed`; the same key with other values is refused and writes nothing. Criterion 4, the data: in `frw-061.prefill-hostile.test.ts` a prefill of five text fields with TeX control sequences (`\textbf`, `\input`, `\write18`, `\def`, `$$`, `\end{document}`), TeX special characters, markup (`<b>`, `<script>`, `]]>`) and spreadsheet formula text (`=HYPERLINK(...)`, `+cmd|...`) is read back equal to what was sent, and its one commit holds each as the string `value` of an attr step and nothing else. Criterion 4, the PDF: `packages/export-latex/src/frw-061-prefill-value.compile.test.ts` (1 test, passed, not skipped: a real sandboxed compile of the export worker image `localhost/docedit-export-worker:s4` in Podman on this machine, 17 s, `fields: 'flatten'`): the same five values as the `value` of text fields give a TeX source in which the values with TeX special characters are not as typed and no `\input{/etc/passwd}` or `\write18{id}` appears, and a PDF whose `pdftotext` shows each value as characters (`BOLDMARK`, `write18`, `<script>`, `=HYPERLINK`), `textbf` and `input` printed as text, no `root:` from the file the value tried to read, and the last paragraph present after the value that tried `\end{document}`. Together with `form-fields.flatten.test.ts` criterion 5 (a field value reaches the TeX only through `text`).

**Notes (lane w4, second pass, 2026-11-18):** All seven criteria are ticked. The stale reasons: criterion 6 waited for the protocol flag, which owner decision 184 settles; criterion 4 waited for a sandboxed compile, which runs here. The earlier test that expected the repeat to be refused by its stale `If-Match` now expects the first answer to be replayed, as the key is now honoured. Outside the lane: `packages/protocol/src/definition/frw.ts`, the regenerated `spec/dep/*`, `docs/spec/05-protocol.md`. `packages/tck/test/mock.test.ts` fails on `authz.actor.opaque` before and after this change (checked by stashing it).

**Notes:** Lane U-FRW-055-072 (2026-11-13): criterion 1 is ticked on the evidence above; the `DEP-Actor` branch of the attribution is not exercised (fixed in round 1 of U3 group 2: both branches are now tested). Round 2: criteria 5 and 7 are ticked (see the Evidence line above). Criterion 4 (TeX and markup escaping on the PDF) stays open: `packages/export-latex` has `form_field` attribute handling but no tested emitter of a field value, so it is not shown here (not decided silently; needs the PDF emitter path). Criterion 6 stays open (see below). Packet C (2026-11-13): criteria 5 and 6 stay open: the accepted path needs a room or a transient authority to apply the commit, and the harness runs neither in the tests that refuse before the outbox. Criterion 4 (TeX and markup escaping on the PDF) is not tested here; criterion 7 was not tested at that point (ticked in round 2, see above). Pitfall (12 §14): prefill as an injection path. Who may call this (a service credential or a user with a capability) and whether prefill is allowed after send are not stated (Open questions). Lane FRW-053-065 (2026-11-13): `services/document-service/src/control-plane/frw-061.prefill-outbox.test.ts` (memory, passes) checks the accepted request as it reaches the outbox: one `prefill` commit, one `value` attr step per key, the base, and the typed text (TeX, markup and formula characters) kept as written. This is the first half of criterion 1 and a part of criterion 4 only; the commit is not applied or stored, and nothing reaches a PDF, so both stay unticked. Criterion 6 is blocked: the protocol definition of `prefillFieldValues` (`packages/protocol/src/definition/frw.ts`) sets no `idempotencyKey`, so the pipeline's Idempotency-Key store does not apply to this route (owner PRO; a root or protocol change, not made here). Lane V-FRW-055-072, round 2 (2026-11-13): criterion 4 stays unticked. The Node part is in place (the prefill's typed value is kept as written, and `form-fields.flatten.test.ts` criterion 5 writes a field value that holds TeX control sequences only through `text`), but nothing in the chain shows a PDF: `packages/export-latex/src/exp-033-field-value.compile.test.ts` skips here because `sandboxReady()` reports that the sandboxed compile cannot run on this machine (run took about five minutes and skipped), so the compile-level proof needs the export image built (needsFromOthers: export worker owner).

**Notes (lane V-FRW-055-072, 2026-11-13, criterion 6 not ticked):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-061.prefill-idempotent.test.ts` applies a prefill through the real data plane, then repeats it with the same `Idempotency-Key` and the same If-Match. It passes in memory and on PostgreSQL (2 passed, not skipped): the repeat is refused with `precondition_failed` (412) and the log holds one commit with origin `prefill`. The key is not replayed, because the protocol definition of the prefill route does not accept `Idempotency-Key` (`packages/protocol/src/definition/frw.ts`; the `idempotencyKey` flag is set only on POST routes, 05 §6.1). The criterion names the key, so it stays open until the owner or the protocol owner decides whether prefill accepts it (proposed, in docs/adr/0342-lane-V-FRW-055-072.md). Criterion 4 (TeX and markup escaping on the PDF) is unchanged.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-17, checked again, nothing new ticked):** criterion 4 still needs a PDF compile, and there is no TeX image here (`podman images` lists only small stub images; the compile test of EXP-033 skips). Criterion 6 is not an owner decision of 2026-11-15 and 05 §6.1 gives `Idempotency-Key` to `POST` routes that create something, while this route is a `PATCH` with a required `If-Match`: today a repeat is refused with 412 and one commit exists, but the key is not accepted or replayed. Making `prefillFieldValues` accept the key is a change to the protocol definition and to 05 §6.1, so it stays with the owner of the protocol (proposed in docs/adr/0342-lane-V-FRW-055-072.md); no code was changed for this story.

### FRW-062 Create a document from a template

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 4 | L | should | Owner |

**Spec:** 12 §9, §10, §14; 05 §6.2 (`/documents`, `/fork`) · **Requirements:** R13 · **Depends on:** FRW-025, FRW-061, UX-099

As an owner, I want to start a document from a template, so that I can reuse role placeholders, fields and rules for every new case.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a template, being a document with role placeholders, fields and rules and no participants, when it is instantiated, then a new document is created as a fork at a pinned `seq` and the `seq` is recorded.
- [x] Given the instantiation, then participants are bound to the roles and values may be prefilled in the same call.
- [x] Given the template changes after instantiation, then existing instances are unaffected.
- [x] Given a template that carries participants or a workflow in a non-draft state, then instantiation refuses or removes them and the new document starts as a draft.
- [x] Given the `/templates` resource, then it supports listing, creating and reading templates, with the schema published in `spec/`.
- [x] Given instantiation, then it needs a service credential, as forking does.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, all criteria):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-062.templates.test.ts` (12 tests: 6 on memory storage and 6 on PostgreSQL in Podman, none skipped; `pnpm exec vitest run --project node <file>`, Node.js 22.18; the real data plane applies the prefill commit). Criterion 1: `POST /documents` with `template` forks the template document; the answer carries `template: { id, seq }` with the head seq, and the new document records `forkedFrom` and `forkedAtSeq`; `templateSeq` pins an earlier seq and the copy holds the content of that seq. Criterion 2: the call binds `p1` and `p2` to the roles `tenant` and `landlord` (the draft workflow of the copy, read with `GET /workflow`) and prefills `tenant.name` and `lease.total` (read with `GET /field-values`), the template keeping no values. Criterion 3: after an instance is made, a prefill on the template changes the template and not the instance. Criterion 4: a template whose workflow is sent (locked, `workflowActive` true) gives a copy with no workflow (404), state `active`, not locked, no grants. Criterion 5: `POST`, `GET /templates` and `GET /templates/{tid}` (201, list, read; a second template of one document is 409; unknown document or template is 404); the schema is published in `spec/dep/dep-1.openapi.json` and `spec/dep/dep-1.schema.json` (generated by `pnpm run generate` in `packages/protocol`; `packages/protocol/test` pass). Criterion 6: a user token is 403 on all three routes and on `POST /documents` with a template. Refusals are whole: an unknown template (404), a role the template lacks, a bad participant, an unknown key and a bad value (400 or 422) create no document (the count of documents is unchanged). Code: `services/document-service/src/control-plane/template-routes.ts`, `services/document-service/src/workflow/template-store.ts` and `template-store-pg.ts`, migration `0093_frw_062_templates.sql`, `packages/protocol/src/definition/frw.ts` and `resources.ts`. The service row of the matrix (`packages/authority/src/permissions/matrix.ts`, `spec/permissions/permissions-1.json`) and the TCK row 13 (`packages/tck/src/profiles/authz.ts`) name the three routes. The decisions are in `docs/adr/0406-w4-FRW-062-templates.md` (proposed); owner decision 184 of 2026-11-15 accepts the workflow design ADRs as a set.

**Notes:** Pitfall (12 §14): a template changed after sending: packets are forks pinned to a template `seq`. The shape of the `/templates` resource and how a document becomes a template are not defined; whether grants are copied is not stated (Open questions). Lane w4, 2026-11-18: all criteria ticked. The shape of `/templates` (a row that marks a document, three service-credential routes) and instantiation by `POST /documents` with `template` are proposals in `docs/adr/0406-w4-FRW-062-templates.md`; grants are not copied. `createDocument` now answers 404 for an unknown template, where it answered `validation_failed` while templates did not exist (`routes.documents.test.ts` changed).

### FRW-063 Send a template to many recipients

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 4 | L | could | Owner |

**Spec:** 12 §9, §3.3 (consequences), §10 · **Requirements:** R13, R9 · **Depends on:** FRW-062, BE-049, BE-092

As an owner, I want to send one template to a list of recipients, so that each gets their own document to fill and sign.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `POST /bulk-sends` with a template, a pinned `seq` and recipient rows, when the job runs, then it creates one fork per row at that `seq`, binds the row's participants and applies the row's prefill values.
- [x] Given a row that fails (an invalid value, an unknown key), then that row is reported with its reason and the other rows are not affected.
- [x] Given the same job request is repeated with the same `Idempotency-Key`, then no fork is created twice.
- [ ] Given tenant quotas, then a request beyond them is refused with `quota_exceeded`.
- [x] Given the job finishes, then its result lists each row with the new document identifier or the failure.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18, criteria 1, 2, 3 and 5):** `services/document-service/test/control-plane/frw-063.bulk-send.test.ts` (10 tests: 5 on memory storage and 5 on PostgreSQL in Podman, none skipped; `pnpm exec vitest run --project node <file>`, Node.js 22.18; the job runs in the service's own runner). Criterion 1: `POST /bulk-sends` answers 202; the job makes one fork per row at the pinned `templateSeq` 0 (the template had changed after it, and the forks lack the change), each recording `forkedFrom` and `forkedAtSeq`; the row's participants are the draft workflow of its document and its values are prefilled (`GET /workflow`, `GET /field-values`). Criterion 2: of five rows an unknown key (`field_invalid` with `/values/no.such.key`), an unknown role (`validation_failed` with `/participants/0/roles`) and a bad value fail with their reasons, and the two good rows are made; the failed rows leave no document. Criterion 3: the same `Idempotency-Key` returns the same bulk send and the count of documents does not change; the same key with another body is 422; the document of each row has an identifier derived from the job and the row (`bulkRowDocumentId`), so a row made already is not made again. Criterion 5: `GET /bulk-sends/{bid}` lists each row with its index and the new document or the failure, and `total` and `done`. A user credential is 403, an unknown template, seq or bulk send 404, an empty or malformed request 400. Code: `services/document-service/src/control-plane/bulk-send.ts`, `template-routes.ts` (`createFromTemplate`, shared with FRW-062), `services/document-service/src/server.ts` (the runner), `packages/protocol/src/definition/frw.ts` and `resources.ts` (published in `spec/dep/`), the service row of the matrix and the TCK row 13. Owner decision 184 of 2026-11-15 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md) accepts the workflow design ADRs as a set.

**Notes:** The row format, the limits and whether each fork is also sent are not stated. Each fork is a full document, so the cost sheet (06 §10) must count it. Lane w4, 2026-11-18: criteria 1, 2, 3 and 5 ticked; the row format, the job and the choices are in `docs/adr/0407-w4-FRW-063-bulk-send.md` (proposed): the forks are drafts and none is sent; a request holds at most 1000 rows (a setting of this build). Criterion 4 stays unticked: the tenant quota dimensions are the seven of 06 §12, fixed in `quotas.ts`, in the `CHECK` of migration 0069 and in the `/quotas` schema, and none limits documents or bulk sends. `quota_exceeded` for a bulk send needs a new dimension and the owner to say what is counted (rows in running bulk sends, rows a month, or documents a tenant may hold); adding it touches 06 §12, a migration of `tenant_quotas`, the `/quotas` schema and its tests. Until then the only limit is the 1000 rows, answered as `validation_failed`.

**Notes (lane w4, 2026-11-26, blocked):** Nothing new is ticked. Criterion 4 still needs the owner to say what a bulk send counts against a tenant quota (rows in running bulk sends, rows a month, or documents held); owner decisions 110 and 134 name the refusals and the quota kinds but do not add this dimension. Adding one would change 06 §12, a migration of `tenant_quotas`, the `/quotas` schema and its tests, so it is not built here.

### FRW-064 Neutralise spreadsheet formula characters in field exports

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | cond | S | should | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 12 §9 (prefill bullet), §14 · **Requirements:** R13, R10 · **Depends on:** FRW-060

As an integrating developer, I want any spreadsheet-format output of field values to neutralise leading formula characters, so that a value typed by a participant cannot run as a formula when opened.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a field value whose first character is `=`, `+`, `-` or `@`, when it is written to a spreadsheet-format output, then the cell is not interpreted as a formula by the spreadsheet applications in the test matrix.
- [x] Given a value with none of those characters, then it is written unchanged.
- [x] Given a `number` field with a negative value, then the output is a number or is neutralised in a way the integrating developer can read back; the behaviour is documented.
- [x] Given the same value read through `GET /documents/{id}/field-values`, then it is returned unchanged, because neutralisation applies only to the spreadsheet output.

**Evidence (lane w4, 2026-11-18):** `services/document-service/src/control-plane/field-csv.ts` (new; `neutraliseCell`, `csvCell`, `fieldValuesCsv`; the rule and the negative-number reading are in its header comment) and `services/document-service/src/control-plane/frw-064.field-csv.test.ts` (vitest, Node.js 22.18, 5 tests, passed with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node`): criterion 2 and the output side of criterion 1 (an apostrophe in front of `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`; quoting of commas and quotes); criterion 3 (a negative number is written `'-12.50`, and one leading apostrophe is removed to read it back); criterion 4 over HTTP in the memory harness (`GET /v1/documents/{id}/field-values` returns `=1+1` as stored, with no apostrophe).
